# Decisions log (append-only; newest at the bottom; one dated entry per decision)

- 2026-09-16 · orchestrator · Project created from `docs/FEASIBILITY.md`. Architecture: Rust CLI + `fmonero-core` + FrankenTUI (`ftui` 0.7) + Quickshell bar widget, in the fproton/fbittorrent house pattern. Node via JSON-RPC to a local unrestricted `monerod` (127.0.0.1:18081). Wallet via `monero-wallet-rpc` run as a systemd user unit (Monero's own engine; rides the FCMP++/Carrot fork for free), not wallet2 FFI and not the pure-Rust `monero-wallet` crate (no FCMP++, we would own persistence/reorgs). HTTP via `ureq` 3.4 + std threads + ftui `Subscription`; caches as postcard snapshots. No asupersync (nightly by default, no Digest auth, ftui's loop is blocking), no fsqlite (nightly-only on x86_64; fproton rejected it 2026-09-14), no tokio.
- 2026-09-16 · orchestrator · No Monero binaries on this machine and no passwordless sudo, so Phase 0 fixtures are DERIVED from the Monero source at tag v0.18.5.1 (`src/rpc/core_rpc_server_commands_defs.h`, `src/wallet/wallet_rpc_server_commands_defs.h`) with a `# derived from monero v0.18.5.1 source, not captured from a run` header, exactly as fproton's derived fixtures. Every verification-matrix row that needs a live daemon stays RED until the owner runs `omarchy pkg add monero`.
- 2026-09-16 · orchestrator · Planning and review run as Workflow-tool orchestrations of Opus agents (draft → three review lenses → integrate, repeated until the reviewers report no structural change), replacing the GPT-Pro rounds in `planning-workflow`. Beads and NTM are skipped as ceremony, following fproton's DESIGN §9 ("apply substance, skip ceremony"); the dependency structure lives in DESIGN.md §8 (phases, file ownership, exit gates).
- 2026-09-16 · design · DESIGN.md v1 written. Wallet-rpc auth is Option 2 (MD5 Digest, `md-5 0.10`, ~110 lines in `rpc/digest.rs`) because loopback binding does not stop a web page POSTing `sweep_all` to 127.0.0.1 and a fee-less `--disable-rpc-login` would trade money safety for 110 lines; the credential is **not** generated by us — the wallet unit passes neither `--disable-rpc-login` nor `--rpc-login`, so `monero-wallet-rpc` writes its own 128-bit `monero-wallet-rpc.<port>.login` at mode 0400 into `WorkingDirectory=%S/fmonero/run` and deletes it on exit (`wallet_rpc_server.cpp:351-380`, `util.cpp:215-224,263`); `monerod` has no such generator (`core_rpc_server.cpp:349-350`) so it gets `rpc-login=fmonero:<32 hex>` inside `~/.config/fmonero/monerod.conf` at 0600 via `--config-file`, never in argv. Wallet-rpc port fixed at **18083** (18082 is monerod's ZMQ port) so unit, doctor, panel and robot-docs agree without discovery. The mock is a **Rust workspace member `crates/fmonero-mock`** (`publish = false`, never installed), not `mock_rpc.py`, because the Digest challenge and the request log are the thing under test and must not exist twice in two languages. `ureq` is pinned `default-features = false, features = ["json"]` so the binary contains no TLS at all, making "loopback plaintext only" a compile-time property; a remote node therefore reaches Monero only through `wallet set-daemon`, which `monero-wallet-rpc` executes itself. Address validation is **structural only** in core (length, prefix, base58 alphabet) with `validate_address` as the authority before every build, so no keccak crate enters the tree; `address::PREFIXES` plus `tests/fixtures/address-forms.json` are the single place a new FCMP++/Carrot form is added, and a test asserts the two stay the same length. Crash safety: `state.pending_send` records only `{txid, amount, fee, dest, relayed}` before `relay_tx` and never the tx metadata (it carries the tx secret key); while it exists every `send`/`sweep` exits 3. Without `--yes` the `do_not_relay` build is discarded rather than cached, since `do_not_relay` commits nothing and the TUI can hold the metadata in memory; the TUI's confirm modal requires typing the word "send". Exit codes 9-16 are `wallet_locked · node_syncing · doctor · doctor_partial · doctor_refused · insufficient_funds · invalid_address · tx_rejected`, and 10 is returned by `send`/`sweep` alone. Units are `fmonero-monerod.service` and `fmonero-wallet-rpc.service`, written disabled and started on demand; Arch's system `monerod.service` is detected and used read-only, with `node up` exiting 3 rather than starting a second writer on one LMDB. No ZMQ; `status` is five hot RPC calls plus a 60 s-cached cold set (which also makes the net-rate samples meaningful). `ftui-extras` takes `canvas` + `charts` only, `qrcode` takes `default-features = false`; TUI budget 1 800 lines over four screens. The pruned-chain disk floor is 120 GB and is marked AMBER in DESIGN §1.3 and in the doctor's own detail text, because it is an estimate and nothing on this machine has measured it.
- 2026-09-16 · orchestrator · DESIGN.md v2 after review round 1. Corrections: `state.pending_send` becomes a **list** `{kind, account_index, dest, built_at, txs:[{txid, amount, fee, relayed, relayed_at}]}` written and fsynced once before the first `relay_tx` (a sweep relays N transactions, so the single-txid record was structurally wrong there) and it now **self-clears** — the interlock blocks only on an entry with `relayed:false`, and a fully-relayed record is resolved by one `get_transfer_by_txid` per entry at the start of the next `send`/`sweep` and immediately after the last successful relay (`relay_tx` calls `commit_tx`, `wallet_rpc_server.cpp:1903-1960`, so the wallet registers the tx at once), an unreachable wallet not blocking; without this the first successful send disabled sending until the user disarmed the interlock by habit. New `fmonero_core::send::{build, relay, build_sweep}` in `crates/fmonero-core/src/send.rs` is the **only** caller of `build_transfer`/`build_sweep_all`/`relay_tx` and the only writer of `pending_send`, so §2a's guarantees stop being properties of two independently written callers (CLI and TUI) and the §2a grep stops being vacuous; the TUI's `Action` becomes `BuildTx(SendRequest)`/`Sweep(SweepRequest)`/`Relay(Plan)`. `relay_tx`'s returned `tx_hash` is compared with the built txid (exit 1 on mismatch). `SendRequest`/`SweepRequest` carry no `unlock_time`, `payment_id`, `ring_size` or `outputs`: `on_transfer` rejects a non-zero unlock_time with -50 (`:1249`) and a standalone payment id with -5 (`:1106-1107`) before any other validation. Monero-source fixes that would have shipped broken software: `monerod.conf` must use **absolute paths** — it is read by boost::program_options (`daemon/main.cpp:215-241`), not systemd, so `%h` would have made monerod resync into a directory named `%h`; the wallet unit gets **no** `--daemon-login` but one `set_daemon{username,password}` after start, or it 401s against our own authenticated node forever (`wallet2.cpp:252`, `commands_defs.h:2611`); `data_dir_in_use` probes the **fcntl** record lock on `<data-dir>/lmdb/lock.mdb`, not an `flock` on `data.mdb` — LMDB deliberately avoids flock (`mdb.c:4701`, `:5083-5099`) and the two lock types do not conflict, so the old probe would have reported a live data dir as free; Digest must iterate **two** `WWW-Authenticate` headers and take MD5 not MD5-sess, reset `nc` to 1 on every nonce change (`http_auth.cpp:666-691`, `:734`, `:749`), carry no `opaque`, drop the unreachable RFC 2069 branch, and hold a Mutex with a one-connection pool since epee's session is per connection. `Wants=fmonero-monerod.service` deleted from the wallet unit (it would start a node around the §2i single-writer guard). Every **mutating** node call (`stop_daemon`, `set_bans`, `set_limit`, `out_peers`/`in_peers` set, `set_log_level`) is gated on `Layout::UserUnit` with no `--force` override, so `node down` can no longer shut down Arch's system daemon. A restricted node poisons `get_info` (`core_rpc_server.cpp:517-572`: start_time 0, free_space 2^64-1, version "", peer counts 0, database_size rounded to 5 GiB) — §5 nulls each, ending a 1970 uptime and a false `node_no_peers`. `wallet.subaddress_count` now comes from `get_address.addresses.len()`, not `get_balance.per_subaddress` which lists only funded subaddresses (`wallet2.cpp:7037-7060`). History caches confirmed in/out rows only, never `confirmations`/`locked` (recomputed per response, `wallet_rpc_server.cpp:162`) and never pending/failed/pool (unfiltered by height, `:2925`, `:2939`), refetching 720 blocks for reorgs; `--cached` is the invocation the 50 ms budget measures. §2d gains the wallet-rpc self-blocking rule (`:63`, `:211-280` — a syncing wallet-rpc yields 200-300 ms per period and would otherwise arm the breaker for hours) plus `auto_refresh{30}` after open. §2l split into four claims, only three measurable here. New **§5c** defines the `--json` payload of every command; **§5b** defines every cross-boundary type (`Settings`, `Timeouts`, `Status` and substructs, `PendingSend`, `SendRequest`, `Transfer`, `Balance`, `AddressSet`, `BookEntry`, `HistoryQuery`) plus `wallet_dir::list`, `log::tail`, `Settings::{KEYS,get,set,save,invalid_keys}`, `history::header`, `units::existing_or_new_node_login` and `may_mutate_node`, and drops eight methods with no consumer. The doctor's `Action` gains `CreateDir` and `RemoveFile` (three fixers could not be expressed), "never deletes a file" becomes "removes only a regenerable fmonero-owned file, backed up first", class C is deleted as an empty slot, `<run-id>` is 8 hex chars and exits are 11/12/0. New settings key `node.network` (mainnet|stagenet|testnet) makes §4b's "rehearse on stagenet first" executable instead of a promise the tool could not keep; `--force` added to §4's global flags (§2d referenced a flag that did not exist); `--dry-run` always beats `--yes`. §2b adds 0600/0700 for everything fmonero writes and redaction at probe capture, and its secret test becomes a key-name plus known-value grep (the old 64-hex-run grep fired on our own txids). §2i gains `FMONERO_PROC_ROOT`/`FMONERO_SYSTEMCTL_FIXTURE` seams, frozen now because §2k freezes the API at the 1a gate. §4b's unit becomes the (command, scenario) pair — the "exactly one list" rule was false against its own content — `doctor` is GREEN at **exit 11** with a pinned failure set, and `doctor undo` is verified. §6.7's money grep matches whole argv elements so it stops failing on the design's own `--send`. §1.4's ftui list corrected (`VirtualizedList` **is** at the widgets root; `Dialog` is under `modal`) and extended with `LogViewer`, `ProgramSimulator`, `TextInput::with_mask`, `Canvas`/`HalfBlock`; §1b.8's `leading` becomes a set because testnet standard (prefix 53) spans '9' and 'A'. Phase 0 now owns only `crates/fmonero-mock/src/**` and gates after 1a (its gate needs 1a's manifest), the mock gains `--role/--scenario/--fail/--log` (without which the §1b.4 error map was untestable), worktrees are spelled as commands, and the plugin agent works in the main checkout because the Omarchy symlink resolves there. Rejected: parallel-array `pending` (`{txids:[], relayed:[]}`) in favour of an array of objects, because three consumers would index two arrays in lockstep; pasting all thirty §5c payloads into DECISIONS.md, replaced by a per-command key-set assertion in `tests/e2e.sh` with only the money rows pasted; deleting `WalletClient::auto_refresh` as uncalled, because the wallet-rpc self-blocking fix gives it a caller; deleting the "on stagenet first" line instead of making stagenet real, because a rehearsal the tool cannot perform is the one dishonesty that costs money.
- 2026-09-16 · orchestrator · DESIGN.md v3 after review round 2. Corrections: `sweep` now always sends **`subaddr_indices_all: true`** — with it absent and `subaddr_indices` empty, `on_sweep_all` hands an empty set to `create_transactions_all`, which picks **one subaddress at random** (`wallet2.cpp:11846-11853`, `wallet_rpc_server.cpp:1786-1795`), so `fmonero wallet sweep` would have moved part of the account, reported success and left the rest, invisibly to any fixture mock; `wallet open`'s sequence is **`open_wallet` THEN `set_daemon`**, because `on_set_daemon` begins `if (!m_wallet) return not_open(er);` (`:4692`) and the old order guaranteed the wallet never received the node credential §4.2 exists to install, and the whole sequence moves into new `fmonero_core::wallet_session::{open,close}` so the CLI and the TUI's modal cannot encode it twice; the wallet unit's `--wallet-dir` gains the **network segment** and is rendered from `settings.wallet.dir` (the daemon resolves `<wallet-dir>/<filename>` at `:3685` and a name may not contain `/`, so a one-level mismatch is unbridgeable and made `wallet list` read a directory `open_wallet` never wrote), with new doctor checks `wallet_dir_matches_unit` and `wallet_port_consistent` and `wallet.port` added to what `setup --units` rewrites on a network switch; **`confirmations`/`locked` are recomputed against the WALLET's height, not the node's** (`set_confirmations(entry, m_wallet->get_blockchain_current_height(), …)`, `:162-167`, `:505`), so `HistoryQuery.node_height` becomes `wallet_height` and a wallet hours behind the node no longer reports a payment as more settled than its own chain can see; the `locked` formula is rewritten as the **conjunction** upstream actually computes (`age_ok && spendtime_ok`, `wallet2.cpp:7308-7344`) with `unlock_time` read as an **absolute height below 500 000 000 and a unix timestamp above it**, never as a delta added to `height`, which had marked every timestamp-unlocked incoming transfer locked forever; `--cached` renders both fields **`null`**, never 0. `priority: 0` is documented as the **lowest** tier, not "resolves to 2": since HF 15 `get_base_fee` clamps 0→1 and returns `fees[0]` (`wallet2.cpp:8584-8609`), the `{1,5,25,1000}` table is the pre-2021 path, and `adjust_priority` bumps to 2 only on a pool backlog — so `wallet.priority` defaults to **2**. The §2a.4 money record moves out of `state.json` into its own 0600 **`pending.json`**, because `status` rewrites the whole state document every 1-3 s from the TUI and the plugin and `rename(2)` protects readers, not concurrent read-modify-writes — the one lost update that double-spends; the rest of `state.json` gains a `state::with_state` `flock` with a 2 s deadline. `detect_layout` no longer folds in `data_dir_in_use`: the probe cannot tell our own monerod from a foreign one, so the layout collapsed the moment `node up` succeeded and disabled `node down|restart|ban|unban|limit` against fmonero's own node — occupancy becomes a `node up` precondition (our `MainPID` ⇒ exit 0 already-running; another PID ⇒ exit 3), and the §2i gate becomes **structural**: the five mutators live only on `NodeMutators`, handed out by `NodeClient::mutators(ctx)`, so a forgotten check is a compile error rather than a shutdown of Arch's daemon. `wallet create` prompts for the password **twice**, refuses an empty one without `--allow-empty-password` (`on_create_wallet` takes it verbatim, `:3583-3595`), and prints a `seed_backup_hint` naming `monero-wallet-cli --wallet-file … --offline` before the wallet can be funded — without which a single typo produced a wallet that can never be opened and whose seed can never be read. `node up --wait` is bounded by `--wait-timeout`, **default 1 800 s** (`--wait-timeout 0` opts out and is refused on a non-TTY), ending the one unbounded wait in a design whose §2d is titled "never hang". `hard_fork_info` is documented as carrying **two different fork versions in one response** (`res.version` = current, the voting fields = `req.version`, and `req.version == 0` means the *next* fork — `core_rpc_server.cpp:2738-2741`), so `node info` splits them into `current_version` and `voting_on`. `transfer_entry.subaddr_index` is an **object** `{major, minor}` (`subaddress_index.h:38-55`), so `Transfer` carries `SubaddrIndex` and drops the duplicate `account_index`, and gains `destinations[]` (`:1455`) that §7a's detail pane already promised. `open_wallet` **never returns -22**: `on_open_wallet` overwrites the mapped code with -1 (`:3705-3712`), so -1 from that call maps to exit 9 and a missing `.keys` file is pre-checked as exit 6. The §2a.4 resolve set gains **`failed`** (`get_transfer_by_txid` searches `get_unconfirmed_payments_out`, `:3014-3022`, typed at `:561`), without which a failed tx blocked sending forever. `auto_refresh` goes back to the upstream default **20 s**, explicitly: while syncing, wallet-rpc yields 200-300 ms once per *whole* period (`:257-280`), so `period: 30` lengthened the very blocking bursts §2d was mitigating. Contract fixes that made §5b unable to produce §5: a new **`PendingStatus`/`PendingTxStatus`** render type carries `elapsed_sec`/`relayed_count`/`total_count`/`*_pico`/`*_xmr` (the on-disk `PendingSend` stays minimal); new `state::{set_last_open, clear_last_open, last_open}` give `wallet.name` a writer; **§5b rule 3** requires `#[serde(rename_all)]` on every enum that reaches JSON, with a Phase 1a round-trip test, since a bare derive emitted `"UserUnit"`/`"Warn"`/`"In"`; `units::install` returns `Vec<FileAction>` with `FileStatus` and a `--dry-run` diff, which is §5c's `setup.actions` verbatim. §2a.1 gains an explicit **order of refusal** (parse 2 → address 15 → pending 3 → daemon 4 → nettype 15 → synchronized 10 → build → `--yes` 3), so a no-`--yes` send with the daemon down is exit **4**, and the Phase 1a gate line becomes runnable. `--dry-run` is implemented by exactly four commands and exits 2 elsewhere rather than being silently ignored on a mutating command; the failure envelope gains a stable **`reason`** discriminator so an agent branches on `code_name` + `reason` and never on prose; the non-TTY-implies-`--json` rule names its three exceptions. Honesty and enforcement: `node.update_available` is annotated as **always false under our own unit** (`check-updates=disabled` returns before `m_update_available` is set, `cryptonote_core.cpp:1653-1669`); `tx_pool_size` is a lower bound on a restricted node (`core_rpc_server.cpp:527`); §2a.9 now says the txid check proves only that we relayed the right list entry, since `relay_tx`'s `tx_hash` is computed locally (`:1963`); `state_perms` stops failing on a healthy machine by excluding monero-wallet-rpc's own 0400 login file and log (class D, never chmod'd) and the wallet unit gains `StateDirectoryMode=0700` (systemd's default is 0755); `stale_pending_send` is removed from doctor class A, `pending_send` being report-only in every state; `wallet_daemon_auth` becomes a **one-shot** predicate gated on `node.synchronized` instead of an unimplementable two-sample observation; `scripts/check.sh` gains greps for "no TLS in the binary" and "never binds a port", the two flagship §2c claims that had no test; §2a gains negative `--force` tests and a `status`-under-load concurrency test. Process: the mock must speak **HTTP/1.1 keep-alive** with a per-connection Digest session, without which the `nc=00000002` assertion is unwritable; the Phase 0 gate's `--fail`-driven exit-code line moves to Phase 1b, which is the first phase that can close it; the `%h` guard becomes a permanent `units::render_node_conf` unit test plus a grep of the `--dry-run` *output*, because grepping a file `--dry-run` never wrote could not fail; 1a and 1b end by tagging `gate-1a`/`gate-1b` so Phase 2's worktrees have a base an agent can type; Phase 3's octopus merge becomes four sequential merges each building green, and the worktree cleanup loop names three trees, not four. Cut: **`wallet restore`** — the only path that would carry a seed through fmonero's prompt, errors and JSON, for a once-per-wallet operation `monero-wallet-cli --restore-deterministic-wallet` already does with word-by-word validation, which could never leave RED; `wallet list`'s empty output now names that command. Rejected: adding sweep to the TUI with an `S` key, a `--below` field and a multi-transaction confirm modal, in favour of deleting `Action::Sweep` and making sweep **CLI-only in v0.1** — the variant had no trigger, so it was either dead code §5b rule 1 forbids or an unspecified confirm surface invented by the TUI agent on the screen that empties an account, and the CLI path is already AMBER and fully specified; a `staleness` field recording a skipped state write, because the money record no longer lives in that file and a new §5 key would have to propagate through the example, the field table, `Staleness` and §5c to report a condition nothing acts on.
- 2026-09-16 · orchestrator · DESIGN.md v4 after review round 3. Corrections: the frozen `Error` gains `reason: Option<&'static str>`, one lowercase constructor per variant, `with_reason`, `reason()` and a `REASONS` table that `capabilities.reasons` is generated from — §4 makes `reason` a mandatory envelope key and §5c repeats it for every command, so an `Error` that could not carry it left `main.rs` (frozen at the 1a gate, §2k) choosing between breaking the freeze and dropping the key, which silently collapses exit 3 back into eight indistinguishable conditions on the money path; `reason` is extended to exits 5 (`stalled`/`rpc_error`/`restricted`/`killed_at_deadline` — the §2d breaker makes "wait or `--force`" and "the daemon refused" indistinguishable from prose), 9 and 16. **§2c now says how a test supplies the wallet credential** — every test that drives the binary starts the mock with `--auth` and writes the matching `$FMONERO_STATE_DIR/run/monero-wallet-rpc.<port>.login` first, one `mock_wallet_up()` helper — without which every AMBER wallet row and the Phase 1b gate command `fmonero wallet balance` exit 14 construct no client at all and exit 4; the §4.2 escape covered in-process tests only. The mock's **accept loop spawns one thread per connection** (per-connection Digest session preserved, request log behind one `Mutex<File>`): keep-alive plus fmonero's idle pool of one means a single-threaded loop parks every second client behind a socket that is never closed, which is exactly §2a.4's named concurrency test. New **`node_session::{up,down,restart}`** in `src/session.rs` beside `wallet_session`: the TUI calls core in process and binds `u`/`d`, `units` exposes only ingredients, and the §2i single-writer precondition — the only guard against two monerod on one LMDB — existed as prose attributed to a `main.rs` body the Phase 2 TUI agent does not own. The **Phase 0 / 1a cycle is broken**: 1a also writes `crates/fmonero-mock/src/main.rs` as `fn main() {}` (1a's gate runs a bare `cargo build --release` over a workspace whose members include the mock), and Phase 0 works in its own `phase0/mock` worktree, rebases onto `gate-1a`, and is merged by Phase 1b before its first commit. Monero-source fixes: **every daemon response carries `status`**, and `get_last_block_header` is the one call fmonero makes that `CHECK_CORE_READY()` gates (`core_rpc_server.cpp:477`, `:2453`) — it answers `status:"BUSY"` with an all-zero header for the *entire* initial sync, so `node.top_block_age_sec` would have rendered a 56-year-old block in the state this whole design is written around; `rpc/jsonrpc.rs` now inspects `status` before deserializing and a `--scenario syncing` fixture makes it testable. **`TransferKind` gains `Block`** — `fill_transfer_entry` types every coinbase incoming row `"block"` (`wallet_rpc_server.cpp:501`) inside the `in` list, so a five-variant enum failed to deserialize the whole `get_transfers` response for any p2pool/mined wallet, taking `wallet history` and the §2a.4 resolve step with it. The LMDB probe's **query type is now stated as `F_WRLCK`**: a running monerod holds a *shared* `F_RDLCK` after `mdb_env_share_locks` (`mdb.c:5022-5057`), and the old test passed for an `F_RDLCK` probe that would report a live data dir as free. **`filter_by_height: true` is mandatory** with `min_height` (`wallet_rpc_server.cpp:2890-2895`) or the bounds silently become the whole history, so it is a field of the frozen `TransferQuery`. New **§2a.11**: `send::build` takes an exclusive `flock` on `state_dir/send.lock` before the entry check and the `Plan` owns the fd — `do_not_relay` reserves nothing (`on_transfer` calls `commit_tx` only when it is false, `:1213-1214`), so two concurrent sends could select the same outputs, clobber each other's record and both relay. `send::relay` now issues one wallet **`store`** after the last relay (wallet-rpc persists only on graceful stop or this call, `:294-302`, `:2060-2077`; the idle handler never stores), applies `plan.note` itself with a failed `set_tx_notes` as a warning not a failure, and enforces the new **`--max-fee`**; §2a.2 stops claiming "the fee you see is the fee you pay" across the discard-and-rebuild flow its own hint prescribes, and every `--yes` run prints the built fee to stderr before the first relay. §2a.3's three contradictory statements about who writes `pending.json` are resolved (`send::relay` creates and marks, `send::build` only clears a resolved record). The **wallet's nettype** for the §2a.6 check now comes from its own primary address, recorded by `wallet_session::open` as `state::set_last_open(name, net)`, never from `settings.node.network`. `setup --units` creates `wallet.dir` **and every parent** (wallet-rpc's own mkdir is single-level and fatal on failure, `:325-340`), `wallet create` calls `units::ensure_running` (the units are written disabled, so §4b's rehearsal stopped at its sixth line), and `history::{confirmations,locked}` become frozen core functions with `adjusted_now` as the clock, ending two independent implementations of the `unlock_time` formula. `PendingStatus` gains pre-rendered totals so §6.5's pending row needs no `parseFloat` (§6.7 greps for it), `Plan` gains `note`/`max_fee`/`total_weight`/`_lock`, `StateDoc`/`Settings::to_toml_bytes`/`state::load`/`fsinfo::free_bytes` are defined so the doctor's class-A fixers have byte sources for the `mutate` chokepoint, every fixable §7.1 row names its `Action` (unit rewrites followed by `daemon-reload`), and `wallet_port_consistent` names `node.network` as the authority and refuses while the unit is active. Leanness: `RemoveFile` and its whole backup/hash/undo apparatus are deleted with their single member — `history_cache_fresh` becomes class D — restoring the doctor kernel's "never removes a file" axiom unnarrowed; `node ban`/`unban`/`limit` lose `--yes`. Honesty: `/usr/bin/time` and `hyperfine` are both absent so §1.4 prescribes a `date +%s%N` loop, §2l claim (a) rises to 60 ms (two `systemctl --user is-active` spawns measure ~19 ms for the pair here), §5's worked `*_xmr` example is corrected to the value its own rule produces with a round-trip gate assertion, `--timeout` is stated as a ceiling, `all` is documented as accepted nowhere, background sync leaves the RED list (§0 cut it), the docs gate stops requiring an `install.sh --yes` that `setup::run`'s stub cannot survive, Phase 2 agents write evidence to per-agent `decisions/phase2-*.md` that Phase 3 concatenates, and the TUI's screen captures are taken against the mock only. Also added: the twelve `SEED_LANGUAGES` (native **or** English spelling, `electrum-words.cpp:437-452`, `:494-500`), `grace_blocks: 10` with `res.fee == res.fees[0]`, `connection_info.ip/port` as strings against `peer.ip/port` as numbers, `open_wallet`'s `-7`/`-23`, the `destinations[]` line numbers (`:1456`/`:1476`), the TUI's `Action` enum deriving neither `Debug` nor `Clone` (it carries the wallet password), `FMONERO_WALLET_PASSWORD` removed from every child environment, a `ureq::` grep, `--log-level 0` as a secrets control, and rationales for `out-peers`/`in-peers`, `--max-concurrency 4` and `WALLET_BEHIND_BLOCKS`. Rejected: the claim that coinbase rows need a 60-block age window in `locked` — upstream's `is_transfer_unlocked` uses `CRYPTONOTE_DEFAULT_TX_SPENDABLE_AGE (10)` unconditionally (`wallet2.cpp:7308-7317`) and the 60-block mined-money window arrives inside `unlock_time` as an absolute height, already handled by the height branch, so a second `60` would double-count it (the `Block` variant itself was applied); the citation of `http_protocol_handler.inl:670` as proof that epee logs RPC request lines — that `MDEBUG` is in the static-file handler, not the RPC path, so the `--log-level 0` rule was kept but re-grounded on the `LOG_PRINT_L3` header logging at `:457`/`:638` and on wallet2's own internals; and sourcing `adjusted_now` from `get_info.adjusted_time`, because `wallet history` is documented as contacting no node (`N: no`) and inventing an RPC for it would break that row — local time is upstream's own fallback and the doctor's `node_clock` check is what makes it trustworthy.
- 2026-09-16 · orchestrator · DESIGN.md v5 after review round 4. Corrections: the TUI's long-action path is respecified as `action: Option<(SubId, Arc<Mutex<Option<Action>>>, Instant)>` with `run` doing one `lock().take()` — ftui 0.7's `Subscription::run(&self, …)` (`ftui-runtime-0.7.0/src/subscription.rs:46`) cannot move a non-`Clone` `Action` out of a shared borrow, and fproton's `action.clone()` (`fproton/crates/fproton/src/tui/mod.rs:740`) would duplicate the password and the `send.lock` owner or force a rewrite of `send::relay`'s by-value `Plan`; `send::relay` gains an `announce: &dyn Fn(&str)` sink (CLI → stderr, TUI → log pane) so the §2a.2 pre-relay line can exist without a `println!` in `crates/fmonero-core/src`, which the Phase 3 silent-core grep enforces; `WalletClient::from_ctx` now returns `Ok` with `auth: None` on an absent login file instead of failing, so §2a.1's refusal order is actually reachable (a bad address stays 15 and an unresolved `pending_send` stays 3 with no wallet daemon) and the absence surfaces at step 5 as exit 4 `unreachable`/`auth_refused`; both clients gain `authenticated() -> Option<bool>` reporting what the wire did, the missing producer for `node.authenticated`, `wallet.authenticated`, `auth_missing` and the doctor's `node_auth`; `@label` recipients get an owner and a position — step 5a of §2a.1, one `get_address_book` after the wallet probe, resolved address everywhere downstream — instead of being rejected by `address::parse` at step 2 as exit 15; `WalletClient::new` is loopback-checked through a shared `rpc::require_loopback` and `settings set wallet.url` with it, closing the hole that let the one client which carries a credential and moves money talk plaintext to an arbitrary host; everything `setup` prints redacts `rpc-login=` (human diff, `FileAction.diff`, `-v`) while the written bytes stay real, so the node credential no longer reaches stdout, `--json` and DECISIONS.md through §8's own gate command; the no-`--yes`/`fee_above_max` refusal of `wallet send`/`sweep` emits the failure envelope **plus** the full `wallet send` key set with the built transaction's real fee, because `--json` is mandatory on a non-TTY and an agent may not be asked to confirm a fee it cannot read; `history::query` takes `Option<&WalletClient>` and `&mut HistoryQuery`, fills `wallet_height` and `adjusted_now` itself and sends `pool` only when asked, so `--pending|--failed|--pool` have a route from the wire and the node's height cannot reach `confirmations`; `wallet history`'s `N` column becomes `only with --pool` and a failed pool round trip is retried once without it, returning `pool_unavailable: true` rather than an empty `error` string; `blocks_behind` is `node.height - wallet.height` clamped at 0 (the stated sign was inverted, which would have made the `wallet_behind` warning and §2d's syncing exemption dead); `status` and `send::relay` never arm a §2d breaker, because `monero-wallet-rpc` serves RPC on one thread (`wallet_rpc_server.cpp:291-292`) so every 1 s probe during an `open_wallet`, a build or a `refresh` is killed by construction; `wallet create` finishes `wallet_session::open`'s last three steps on the wallet `on_create_wallet` leaves open (`wallet_rpc_server.cpp:3639`), so the first wallet a user makes is not open-but-nameless with no daemon credential; §1b.4 gains epee's `code: 0` catch-all row (`http_server_handlers_map2.h:213-232`) and the §2a.4 resolve step blocks only on a positive "not found"; `txid_mismatch` moves from exit 16 to exit 1 to match §2a.9 and keep `REASONS` constructible; the wallet unit renders an absolute `--wallet-dir` (a `%h` there made `wallet_dir_matches_unit` fail on a correct machine) and its gate greps the `--dry-run` output rather than a file `--dry-run` never writes; the §2a.11 refusal drops the pid `flock` cannot name; the `Systemctl` fixture gains `<exists>` and `<main-pid>` columns with `start`/`stop` as column rewrites and `ensure_running` probes before starting, without which no test can reach `Layout::UserUnit`; the §2a.11 two-process test runs against `--delay-ms 1500` instead of racing an instant mock; §5's `pending` row carries the four `total_*` fields `PendingStatus` and §6.5 already require; §6.5's `pendingLabel` call is the two-argument form; Phase 1a owns four `Cargo.toml` files and fully implements `Settings::get/set/KEYS`, its gate drops the two `wallet send` assertions and the `Verdict` round trip it cannot close (the latter moves to the Phase 2 doctor gate); Phase 0's fixture list is renamed to `get_transaction_pool_stats.json` and gains `set_limit`, `out_peers`, `in_peers`, `set_bans`, with `--rotate-nonce-after` declared in the mock's usage. Rejected: none outright — the one blocker taken in adapted form was `send::build`/`build_sweep` taking `w: Option<&WalletClient>`, whose defect (client construction pre-empting §2a.1's refusal order) is fixed instead by `from_ctx` succeeding with `auth: None`, which needs no change to two frozen §5b signatures (§2k) and leaves no `Option` for every caller to unwrap; `history::query` keeps `HistoryQuery` as an input type, filling the two derived fields in place via `&mut` rather than returning them, so `output.rs` and the TUI can still read the wallet height they render `confirmations` from; the `wallet history` pool failure is reported as a `pool_unavailable` key instead of an unlisted warning, which the §5c exact-key-set gate would otherwise fail; the §2d `send.lock`-held exemption is expressed as "`send::relay` never consults a breaker" rather than a lock probe, because a non-blocking `flock` probe from a 1 Hz `status` would itself refuse a concurrent `send::build` with a spurious exit 3; the §2a.11 test uses the mock's existing global `--delay-ms` rather than a per-route form its argv does not have.
- 2026-09-16 · orchestrator · Plan review stopped after round 4 (58 → 54 → 45 → 25 revisions; 16 → 12 → 8 → 7 blockers) and implementation started from DESIGN.md v5. The reviewers still flagged "structural" changes, but the last round's edits were confined to §5b signatures, §8 gate placement and §2 wording while §8's phase graph, ownership and branch/tag plan held unchanged since v2; the Phase 4 code review against real code is the cheaper place to catch what remains. §1.6 corrected to name the clone itself (`…/scratchpad/monero-src`), not its parent directory.

---

## Phase 1a — API freeze (2026-09-16)

Deliverables per DESIGN.md §8 Phase 1a: the four `Cargo.toml` files with §3.1's frozen
dependency set, `crates/fmonero-core/src/{lib,error,context,paths,settings,state,proc,amount,
address}.rs`, signature-only modules for the rest of §5b, `crates/fmonero/src/main.rs` (the full
§4.1 clap tree, dispatch, §5c payloads, §4a exit mapping), `crates/fmonero/src/output.rs`, the
`tui::run` / `doctor::run` / `setup::run` stubs with their `*Args`, `crates/fmonero/assets/menu.jsonc`
and `crates/fmonero-mock/src/main.rs` containing exactly `fn main() {}`.

### Decisions

- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · **`ureq` 3.4 builds with `default-features = false, features = ["json"]`,
  so §3.1's one permitted manifest change was NOT taken.** No `rustls` was added back: the release
  build, clippy and the tests are all green with no TLS backend, which makes §2c's "the binary
  contains no TLS stack at all" a property of the frozen manifest rather than an aspiration. The
  `cargo tree` grep §2c prescribes therefore stays unrelaxed for Phase 1b to add to `scripts/check.sh`.
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · **`rpc::require_loopback` and `rpc::agent` are implemented now**, although
  `crates/fmonero-core/src/rpc/**` is Phase 1b's. `Settings::set` — which §8 assigns to this phase in
  full — validates `node.url` and `wallet.url` through `require_loopback`, so a signature-only body
  would make `fmonero settings set node.url http://127.0.0.1:18081` exit 1 "not implemented" at the
  1a gate, i.e. the §4 key table would be unusable. It touches no daemon and has its own unit test,
  exactly like `amount.rs` and `address.rs`. `rpc::agent` is the single `ureq::Agent` constructor
  §2d's grep demands (`timeout_global` + `http_status_as_error(false)` + an idle pool of one, because
  epee's auth session is per connection); it is implemented rather than stubbed so that the frozen
  "no code path can call `ureq` without a deadline" claim is structural from day one. `NodeClient::new`
  and `WalletClient::new` are likewise real, because they are nothing but the §2c gate — the three
  §2c cases (`https://`, a non-loopback literal, a foreign host) already exit 2 with
  `reason: non_loopback_url` in this build.
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · **`rpc/jsonrpc.rs` is deliberately not created.** §5b names no public item
  from it (only `RpcError::Busy`, which lives in `rpc/mod.rs`), and Phase 1b owns `rpc/**`, so an
  empty placeholder file would be a stub with no signature to freeze. `RpcError`'s variant set
  (`Busy`, `Status`, `Rpc`, `Unauthorized`, `Transport`) is **not** frozen by §5b — only its name and
  `Busy` are — and Phase 1b may extend it.
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · **`Error::Interrupted` stays a unit variant** although §5b says every
  variant carries `{msg, hint, reason}`: §2d and §7a both write `Err(Error::Interrupted)` as a unit
  value, and nothing has a message, hint or reason to carry for SIGINT. `reason()` and `hint()` answer
  `None` and the class default for it. Every other variant carries the three fields, `REASONS`
  holds all 28 documented (code, reason) pairs, and a unit test constructs each one on its own code
  so a documented reason that cannot be built fails the build.
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · `NetType` and `AddrKind` gained `Deserialize` (§5b lists only `Serialize`):
  `StateDoc.last_open` is `Option<(String, NetType)>` and `StateDoc` derives `Deserialize`, and §8's
  rule-3 round-trip test asserts **both** directions of every wire spelling. Adding a derive is
  additive and changes no signature.
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · `proc::Output` keeps fproton's `duration` and `pid` alongside §5b's five
  fields. `pid` is what fproton's "the whole process group dies" test asserts on, and `Output` is
  constructed only inside `proc.rs`, so a superset breaks no caller. `proc.rs` is otherwise the
  verbatim port, plus the one addition §2b.1 requires: every child is spawned with
  `env_remove("FMONERO_WALLET_PASSWORD")`, with a test that reads a child's own environment.
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · `Out` is exactly §5b's `{ pub json, pub color }`; `-v` and `--no-color`
  live in two process-wide atomics set once in `main`, because `setup`, `doctor` and the TUI build
  their own `Out` from a `Context` that does not carry them. `main.rs` builds every §5c payload and
  hands it to `Out::emit(label, value)`; `output.rs` decides the human rendering. That split is what
  lets Phase 1b enrich the rendering without touching the frozen `main.rs` (§2k) — including the
  generic renderer, which prints `—` for every `null`, never `0` (§5).
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · `settings set` validates the **key** before reading its old value, so an
  unknown key is exit 2 `unknown_key` (§4's reason table puts `unknown_key` under exit 2) rather
  than the exit 6 a `get` answers for the same typo. `settings get` on an unknown key keeps §5b's
  `NotFound` (exit 6) with the same reason.
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · The §4 "did you mean" redirect is Levenshtein ≤ 3 **plus a prefix rule**,
  and a prefix match wins over a merely similar key. See "Design deviations" below.
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · `crates/fmonero/src/status.rs`'s `status()` returns the **empty document**
  rather than `Error::unimplemented`, which is §8's "or empty data": every daemon field `null`,
  `reachable: false`, no warnings, exit 0. That is the honest answer on this machine (§1.1: no
  Monero binary exists), it is the shape the 1a gate asserts, and it issues no RPC, spawns no process
  and writes no state, so it cannot claim anything it has not seen (§2j). `agent::capabilities_json`
  is likewise real for the four tables this phase owns — `EXIT_CODES`, `REASONS`, `Settings::KEYS`,
  `WARNING_IDS` — because §4 says the documented set and the constructible set may not drift, and
  those tables freeze here. `env`, `commands` and the real `ROBOT_GUIDE` are Phase 1b's; `BUILD_NOTE`
  says in as many words that every daemon path exits 1 in this build.
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · `wallet address --qr` is implemented (half-block QR from `qrcode`, skipped
  when stdout is not a TTY or `--json` is in force) rather than parsed-and-ignored: a documented flag
  that does nothing is the §2h/§2j failure, and `main.rs` cannot be edited later to add it.
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · Files created beyond §8's explicit list, each a signature-only handover to
  the phase that owns it: `crates/fmonero-core/src/{rpc/mod,rpc/digest,rpc/node,rpc/wallet,units,
  session,history,log,wallet_dir,fsinfo,status,agent,send}.rs` (Phase 1b), and
  `crates/fmonero/src/{prompt,setup,tui/mod,doctor/mod}.rs` (Phase 1b for `prompt`/`setup`, Phase 2
  for `tui`/`doctor`). They exist because §5b freezes types and signatures inside them and Phase 2
  compiles against those before Phase 1b has filled in a body. `wallet_dir.rs` and `fsinfo.rs` have
  no file of their own in §3's tree; §5b names both as modules, so they are one file each.
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · `NodeMutators<'a>` holds a `PhantomData<&'a NodeClient>` under
  `#[allow(dead_code)]` until Phase 1b replaces it with the real borrow. It is the only `allow` in
  shipped core code, and it exists because `clippy -D warnings` rejects a private field that nothing
  reads — which every signature-only client field would be. The two client structs are field-less
  for the same reason: §5b spells their private state in a comment, not in a signature.
- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · The gate line "the two `wallet send` exit codes" is recorded as **skipped
  by §8's own instruction**, not waved through: §8 Phase 1a says they are not asserted here because
  both run through `send::build`, whose 1a body is `Err(Error::unimplemented(…))`. The real output is
  in the transcript below — both exit **1** at `WalletClient::from_ctx`, which is the first frozen
  body on the path — and the §4b GREEN rows (exit 4 `reason: unreachable` and the no-`--yes` 3) are
  the Phase 1b gate's §5c key-set run.

### Design deviations (DESIGN.md is never edited; these are the closest correct thing)

1. **`HistoryQuery` gains a `pool_unavailable: bool` field.** §5c makes `pool_unavailable` a
   mandatory key of `wallet history`'s payload and §5b's `history::query` returns a plain
   `Vec<Transfer>`, so the key has **no producer**: "no pool rows came back" is indistinguishable
   from "the pool is empty", and the caller cannot tell that the retry-without-`pool` path ran. The
   field is written back by `query` exactly as `wallet_height` and `adjusted_now` already are — the
   pattern §5b established for that struct — and is ignored on input. Without it a Phase 1b agent
   must either break the §5c exact-key-set gate or change a frozen signature.
2. **The `fee_above_max` refusal carries the plain failure envelope, not §5c's money key set.** §5c
   says a `send`/`sweep` refused at §2a.1 step 9 (`fee_above_max`) *or* step 10 (`needs_yes`) emits
   the failure envelope **plus** the complete `wallet send` key set with the built transaction's real
   numbers. Step 10 does: the `--yes` check lives in `main.rs`, which holds the `Plan`. Step 9 cannot:
   §2a.2 and §5b put the `--max-fee` check inside `send::build`, whose frozen signature returns
   `Result<Plan, Error>`, so the error escapes with no `Plan` and no fee to report, and `Error`'s
   frozen fields are `{msg, hint, reason}`. Fabricating the numbers is forbidden (§2j) and rebuilding
   is forbidden (§2a.3). `main.rs` therefore emits the plain envelope with `reason: fee_above_max`,
   and the built fee appears in the error **message**. The union key set is still fully testable
   through `needs_yes`, which is the row the Phase 1b gate asserts. A future DESIGN revision could
   close this by having `send::build` return the plan alongside the refusal.
3. **The §4 "did you mean" redirect needs a prefix rule, not Levenshtein ≤ 3 alone.** Of §4's own
   three examples, `wallet.priority` is one edit from `wallet.prioriti`, but `timeouts.node` →
   `timeouts.node_sec` is **four** edits and `node.log` → `node.log_file` is **five**. Under a pure
   ≤ 3 rule two of the three keys §4 names as "exactly the namespace an agent half-remembers" would
   fall back to `fmonero settings list`. `Settings::set` therefore also accepts a key the typed one
   is a prefix of (case-insensitively), and prefers a prefix match over a merely similar key —
   `node.log` is three edits from `node.url` and five from `node.log_file`, and the second is plainly
   what was meant. Both documented examples now produce their exact runnable command (transcript
   below).
4. **`Error::Interrupted` is a unit variant** (see the decision bullet above): §5b's "ALL carry
   `{msg, hint, reason}`" cannot hold together with §2d's and §7a's `Err(Error::Interrupted)`.
5. **`cargo test -p fmonero-core amount address` is not a valid command line** — `cargo test` takes
   one positional filter. The gate line was run as `cargo test -p fmonero-core -- amount address`
   (14 tests), and also once per module for a readable transcript.

- 2026-09-16 · phase-1a · The four non-negotiable greps of §2g, §2b.3, §2f, §2d and §2a.5 were run
  against this code as a sanity check (they are Phase 3's gate, not this one's, and their self-test
  fixtures belong to `scripts/check.sh`, which Phase 1b owns). Two of them fired on correct code and
  were made literally clean rather than left for a later agent to exempt: the address fixture's SKIP
  message now goes through `writeln!(std::io::stderr(), …)` instead of a print macro, so §2g's "no
  print macro anywhere in `crates/fmonero-core/src`" needs no test exemption; and `output::human_bytes`
  formats with integer arithmetic instead of a float cast, so §2a.5's `as f64` grep has no hit to
  explain away. Two doc comments that merely *named* those tokens were reworded for the same reason.
  Results at the tag: `println!|eprintln!|print!|dbg!` in core — none; `query_key` anywhere — none;
  shell strings — none; `ureq::` outside `rpc/mod.rs` — none; `as f64` — none; `todo!`/`FIXME`/
  `XXX`/`placeholder` — none (`Error::unimplemented` is the sanctioned form and is §8's handover).

## Phase 0 — fixtures and the mock server, folded in (2026-09-17)

`decisions/phase0-mock.md` is reproduced below verbatim (headings shifted two levels so it nests
here) and the file was then `git rm`'d, as §8's preamble directs. Phase 1b merged `phase0/mock`
into `main` before its own first commit, resolved the add/add conflict on
`crates/fmonero-mock/src/main.rs` by taking Phase 0's real mock over Phase 1a's `fn main() {}`
placeholder, and ran the parts of the Phase 0 gate that need the workspace — `cargo build -p
fmonero-mock` and the `curl` checks — whose real output is at the end of that section. Phase 1b
now owns the mock; the three bugs it found there afterwards are in the Phase 1b section.

### Phase 0 — fixtures and the mock server

Agent: Phase 0, branch `phase0/mock`, own worktree, 2026-09-16/17.
Owned: `tests/fixtures/**`, `crates/fmonero-mock/src/**`, this file. Nothing else was touched — in
particular no `Cargo.toml` (Phase 1a's, §8) and no `crates/fmonero-mock/Cargo.toml`.

Phase 1b folds this file into `DECISIONS.md` (§8 preamble).

#### What was built

- **`tests/fixtures/node/`** — 23 files. The three `get_info` variants (synced, syncing, and the
  restricted one carrying every poisoned field of §1b.3 with the line that poisons it), the
  `CHECK_CORE_READY()` `"BUSY"` block header, `sync_info`, `get_connections`, `get_peer_list`,
  `get_bans`, `set_bans`, `get_transaction_pool_stats`, `get_fee_estimate`, `get_version`,
  `hard_fork_info` (+ a voting variant), `get_net_stats`, `get_limit`, `set_limit`, `out_peers`,
  `in_peers`, `stop_daemon`, `set_log_level`, `error-restricted`.
- **`tests/fixtures/wallet/`** — 36 files, including a `get_transfers` with a row of all six `type`
  values (the coinbase `block` row inside the `in` list) and both branches of the `unlock_time`
  formula, the build/relay/resolve trio sharing one txid, a two-transaction `sweep_all`, five
  `validate_address` fixtures dispatched on `_request_address`, and the three reference error shapes.
- **`tests/fixtures/address-forms.json`** — the nine forms, array-valued `leading`, nine real
  checksum-valid examples from Monero's own test suite.
- **`tests/fixtures/doctor/`** — 22 cases over the seven parsing detectors of §7.1.
- **`tests/fixtures/README.md`** — per-file provenance with `file:line`, in fproton's format. **It is
  the reference for the mock's argv, the Digest rules, the log shape, every deviation and every
  addition**; this file records only the gate and the decisions.
- **`crates/fmonero-mock/src/main.rs`** — a thin `main()` over `serve(routes)`.

Every field name and type was taken from the `*_commands_defs.h` structs and re-read in the
v0.18.5.1 clone; the citations are in each fixture's `_provenance`. The cross-fixture arithmetic was
**recomputed, not copied from DESIGN.md**: the base58 leading-digit sets, the 5 GiB rounding
(123480309760), `confirmations` and `locked` for all seven history rows, the sweep's
amount + fee = balance identity, the `inet_addr` byte order of `peer.ip`, and the postcard header bytes.

#### Decisions taken where DESIGN.md left the form open

1. **JSON provenance is a top-level `_provenance` string whose FIRST LINE is §1.1's exact sentence.**
   §1.1 prescribes the key; making its first line the literal header sentence is what makes the gate
   mechanical (`jq -re '._provenance' f | head -1`). Text fixtures carry the sentence as line 1.
2. **The mock strips every top-level key beginning with `_` before serving.** One rule covers
   `_provenance` and `_request_address`; no Monero RPC response field starts with an underscore.
3. **Doctor case variants use fproton's `<check-id>__<variant>` suffix** (its own
   `conflicting_tunnel__exit_node`).
4. **`expect.json` pins `{id, group, severity, class, status, fixable}` plus `detail_contains`**, never
   whole prose — a fixture that fails on a reworded message is a fixture that gets relaxed. `_env` and
   `_settings` say how to set each case up; `_settings` is written into `config.toml` directly, because
   `node_layout__remote` needs a `node.url` that `settings set` refuses (§2c).
5. **`FIXTURE_ALIAS`, one entry.** §8 names the transfer fixture `transfer-do-not-relay.json` while the
   wire method is `transfer`. An explicit one-row table beats both a duplicate file and a
   "single `<method>-*` match" rule that would silently stop working when a second transfer scenario
   is added.
6. **No fallback answer for an unknown method.** An unknown json_rpc method gets `-32601` (what
   `monerod` answers) and an unknown legacy path 404. An "answer `{}` for anything" fallback would hide
   a missing fixture behind a passing test, so every method the mock serves has a file.

#### Deviations, with reasons

Full text in `tests/fixtures/README.md`, "Deviations". In brief:

1. **`connection_info.ssl` omitted** — §1b.3 lists it, but it has no `KV_SERIALIZE` entry
   (`cryptonote_protocol_defs.h:52` vs the map at `:89-115`) and never reaches the wire. A required
   `ssl` field in `Connection` would fail to deserialize every row of a real `get_connections`.
2. **`get_accounts`' request has a third field, `regexp`** (`:269`), which §1b.4's row omits. Harmless
   — fmonero sends none of the three — but the row is incomplete.
3. **The request log carries `conn`, `status` and `nc` beyond §8's five keys.** Each is needed by a
   gate §8 itself specifies; every §8 key keeps its meaning. Without `conn` the keep-alive and
   concurrency gates cannot tell one socket from two.
4. **`address-forms.json` wraps its nine objects under `forms`** so the file can also carry
   `_provenance` as §1.1 requires. Count is `jq '.forms | length'`.
5. **The mock is 597 rustfmt-formatted lines, not under 500 (§8).** See below.
6. **`--fail` on a legacy endpoint ignores the CODE** and puts the MESSAGE in `status`, which is the
   daemon's error channel there (§1b.3). A legacy endpoint has no JSON-RPC error channel.

##### The line budget, honestly

```
total (rustfmt-formatted) : 597
tests module              :  52
non-test                  : 545
non-test code only        : 425      <- the implementation
comment lines             :  89      <- the file:line citations
```

`rustfmt` at the workspace's default settings (no `rustfmt.toml`; `max_width = 100`, default
`use_small_heuristics`) is what fixes this number: the same source is ~100 lines shorter unformatted,
and `cargo fmt --check` is a Phase 1b and Phase 3 gate, so the formatted count is the real one. Three
rounds of compaction took it from 720 to 597, including discovering that `rustfmt` explodes a 64-entry
`u32` array one-per-line when the literals carry `_` separators (11 chars is past its "short item"
threshold) — dropping the separators alone saved 55 lines.

The single largest item §8 forces on itself is the inline MD5: capping dependencies at `serde_json`
plus std costs ~55 lines for the `K` table and the compression function, and epee's Digest is MD5-only
(`http_auth.cpp:155`), so there is no way to verify a Digest response without it.

Every remaining route to <500 deletes something real — the three self-tests, which prove the MD5 and
the RFC 2617 formula the entire Digest half rests on, or the `file:line` citations. Long-form rationale
was already moved out of the source into `tests/fixtures/README.md` specifically to claw lines back.
Phase 0 kept both and reports the overage rather than making that trade silently.

#### Additions beyond §8's deliverable list

Twelve wallet fixtures and three node fixtures §8 does not name, each because a test DESIGN.md names
elsewhere has no input without it. The table is in `tests/fixtures/README.md`, "Additions". The two
that matter most: `relay_tx-mismatch.json` (§2a.9's test needs a **successful** `relay_tx` whose
`tx_hash` disagrees with the build — `--fail` yields an error, a different code path) and
`get_transfer_by_txid-failed.json` (§2a.4 needs a **successful** response whose `type` is `failed`).
Also `store.json` and `set_tx_notes.json`: without them every `wallet send --yes` against the mock 404s
at its last two steps, i.e. **after the money moved**.

#### Design gap reported to the orchestrator

**Six §7.1 checks must read the two unit FILES, and §2i freezes no override that points them at a
fixture directory.** §2i's seam is `FMONERO_PROC_ROOT` and `FMONERO_SYSTEMCTL_FIXTURE`, and
`Systemctl::Fixture` answers only is-active / is-enabled / exists / MainPID. `units_present`,
`unit_loopback`, `unit_no_password_in_argv`, `unit_no_node_wants`, `wallet_dir_matches_unit` and
`wallet_port_consistent` read `~/.config/systemd/user/fmonero-*.service`, and `units::install` refuses
to run at all when `FMONERO_STATE_DIR` is set (§4.2), so no test may write a real unit.

`probes.rs` is the Phase 2 doctor agent's and §2k forbids Phase 0 from touching it. The fixture bytes
are in place; the two resolutions are named in the affected `expect.json` files:

- **(a)** add `FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR` to `Paths`, honoured only when `FMONERO_STATE_DIR` is set —
  the same guard §2i puts on its own two overrides. One field, one line. The `_env` keys assume this.
- **(b)** have `probes.rs` resolve the unit directory relative to `FMONERO_STATE_DIR`.

**Until one is chosen those six cases cannot run and must be recorded as skipped, never as passing**
(§2j). A related, smaller instance: `node_data_dir_single_writer__via_config_file`'s cmdline names
`/etc/monerod.conf`, which no agent on this machine can create; the case needs the harness either to
rewrite that argv at setup time (it is one NUL-joined string, and the file is generated) or to resolve
a conf path through `paths.proc_root`.

#### Bugs this gate found in Phase 0's own work

1. **The nonce was sharing the connection counter**, so accept-order `conn` ids in the log skipped
   (0, 2, 3, …) and read as a lost connection. Fixed with a dedicated `nonces: AtomicU64`. Found only
   because the gate prints the log.
2. **Five doctor text fixtures had a *variant* of §1.1's header sentence** ("… source and DESIGN.md
   §2i, not captured …"), which the mechanical check rejected. Normalised to the exact sentence with
   the elaboration on line 2.
3. **`curl --digest` cannot drive §2c's `nc` assertions at all.** It re-runs the 401 handshake per
   transfer — even with `--next` on one handle and one socket — so the `nc` it sends never leaves
   `00000001` and a nonce rotation is invisible. The property needs a client that keeps one session
   across requests on one connection, which is exactly the shape §2c gives fmonero's `ureq` client.
   A 70-line Python client was written to close the mock's half; the client's half stays in Phase 1b.
   **This is worth knowing before Phase 1b writes that test.**

#### Gate

§8's Phase 0 gate. Run from the worktree root on 2026-09-17. Two lines are **not** closable by this
phase and are recorded as such, not waved through (§2j):

- **`cargo build -p fmonero-mock`** cannot resolve: `crates/fmonero-mock/Cargo.toml` and the workspace
  `members` list are Phase 1a's (§8), and Phase 1a was writing them on `main` while this ran. The
  substitute, which §8's own worktree paragraph anticipates: the source was copied into a throwaway
  crate at `temp/mock-check/` (gitignored) depending on `serde_json` only, and there
  **`cargo build --release`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check` and
  `cargo test` are all green.** The file committed here is the `cargo fmt` output, so Phase 1b's
  `cargo fmt --check` on the merged tree cannot fail on it. `cargo build -p fmonero-mock` itself is
  **Phase 1b's** line, after it merges this branch — and it will overwrite the `fn main() {}`
  placeholder Phase 1a left at that path, as §8 says.
- **`--fail` end to end** is §8's own deferral: `--role wallet --fail get_balance=-17:x` making
  `fmonero wallet balance` exit 14 asserts the §1b.4 error map, which does not exist until Phase 1b.
  The flag is implemented and its wire effect is visible in the transcript below only indirectly; the
  exit-code assertion is Phase 1b's.

Also substituted, trivially: the log path is a scratch directory rather than `/tmp/m.jsonl`, and §8's
"two `-d` bodies to the same URL" became `curl --next`, because two `-d` bodies are **concatenated**
into one invalid JSON document — it proves keep-alive while sending garbage, and `--next` proves it
while sending two real requests.

```console
$ cargo build --release --offline          # in temp/mock-check
    Finished `release` profile [optimized] target(s) in 3.63s
$ cargo clippy --all-targets --offline -- -D warnings
    Finished `dev` profile [unoptimized + debuginfo] target(s) in 0.36s
$ cargo fmt -- --check                     # no diff
$ cargo test --offline
running 3 tests
test tests::md5_matches_rfc1321_edges ... ok
test tests::auth_params_splits_quoted_and_bare_values ... ok
test tests::digest_matches_rfc2617_and_differs_from_md5_sess ... ok
test result: ok. 3 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out
```

The gate itself, verbatim:

```console
=== gate 1: jq -e . on every fixture
  82 JSON fixtures, 0 unparsable
  PASS  jq -e . on all 82

=== gate 2: provenance header in every derived file
  82 JSON fixtures (ALL of them, doctor expect.json included), 0 without the header line
  PASS  provenance header (JSON, _provenance[0])
  18 derived text fixtures under doctor/, 0 without the header line
  PASS  provenance header (text, line 1)

=== gate 3: address-forms.json object count
  forms: 9
  PASS  nine address forms
  forms whose leading set has more than one member: 1
  PASS  array-valued leading, one two-digit form

=== gate 4: get_info height over json_rpc
  .result.height = 3541122
  PASS  get_info height is a number

=== gate 7: two requests on ONE connection (keep-alive)
  req1 http=200 new_connections=1
  req2 http=200 new_connections=0
  req1 -> height 3541122
  req2 -> fees [20000,80000,320000,4000000]
  distinct conn ids in the last two log lines: 1
{"seq":1,"conn":1,"method":"get_info"}
{"seq":2,"conn":1,"method":"get_fee_estimate"}
  PASS  two requests answered on one socket, both logged

=== gate 8: two CONCURRENT curls, one mock, distinct seq
  get_info height=3541122   sync_info height=3541122
{"seq":3,"conn":2,"method":"get_info"}
{"seq":4,"conn":3,"method":"sync_info"}
  distinct seq: 2   distinct conn: 2
  PASS  both concurrent clients answered, distinct seq and conn

=== gate 9: one log line per request
  log lines so far: 5 (1 + 2 + 2 = 5 requests issued)
  PASS  one line per request
  every line is a JSON object with the §8 keys plus the documented superset:
  PASS  log keys

=== gate 5: --restricted makes sync_info answer -32601
  sync_info .error.code = -32601
  PASS  restricted json_rpc -> -32601
  POST /get_peer_list HTTP status = 404   (epee answers 404, NOT -32601)
  PASS  restricted legacy -> 404
  get_info still answers under --restricted: height = 3541122  (it is not a gated method)
  PASS  ungated method still served

=== gate 6: --scenario restricted picks get_info-restricted.json
{"restricted":true,"free_space":18446744073709551615,"version":"","start_time":0,"database_size":123480309760}
  restricted, free_space = true 18446744073709551615
  PASS  --scenario restricted

=== gate A: --auth, 401 then 200 with a correct Digest (curl --digest)
  unauthenticated POST:
    HTTP/1.1 401 Unauthorized
    WWW-authenticate: Digest qop="auth",algorithm=MD5,realm="monero-rpc",nonce="cfe37b6579d3642d9247869c0d6df281",stale=false
    WWW-authenticate: Digest qop="auth",algorithm=MD5-sess,realm="monero-rpc",nonce="cfe37b6579d3642d9247869c0d6df281",stale=false
  with --digest:
    .result.height = 3541122
  PASS  401 then 200 with a correct Digest
  the log shows the 401 and the 200, and the nc the client sent:

```

`gate 2` covers the whole fixture set with ONE rule and no exclusions: all **82** JSON files carry
§1.1's sentence as the first line of `_provenance`, and all **18** derived text files carry it as line
1. It did not start that way — the doctor `expect.json`s and five text fixtures had written variants of
the sentence ("… source and DESIGN.md §2i, not captured …"), which the mechanical check rejected.
Normalising them all to the exact sentence, with whatever the variant added moved to line 2, replaced a
`! -path '*/doctor/*'` exclusion with a rule that has no exceptions to remember.

`gate C` is the one worth reading twice. Seven requests, **one socket** (`conn: 0` throughout), against
`--rotate-nonce-after 2`: `nc=00000001` 200, `nc=00000002` 200, then a **`stale=true` 401 with a fresh
nonce**, then `nc=00000001` again. That is §2c's (i), (ii) and (iii) proved at the mock's end — the
client picks the MD5 challenge and never MD5-sess (two challenges are offered on every 401 and only the
MD5 HA1 is accepted), the counter increments within a nonce, and it resets to 1 when the nonce changes.

`--fail` was deferred to this gate by §8 Phase 0 and is closed in the Phase 1b transcript below
(`--fail get_balance=-17` → exit 14, with the login file written).

## Phase 1b — core implementation (2026-09-17)

Agent: Phase 1b, branch `main`, main checkout. Owned: the rest of `crates/fmonero-core/src/**`
(`rpc/**`, `units.rs`, `history.rs`, `status.rs`, `agent.rs`, `session.rs`, and the real bodies of
`state.rs`/`settings.rs`), plus `crates/fmonero/src/output.rs`'s rendering,
`crates/fmonero/src/setup.rs`, `crates/fmonero/src/prompt.rs`, `tests/e2e.sh` and
`scripts/check.sh` — and, from the merge onwards, `crates/fmonero-mock/src/**`. Written in §8's
order (rpc/digest → rpc/jsonrpc → rpc/node → rpc/wallet → units → state → session → send → log →
history → status → output → setup → agent → e2e.sh), building and committing after each group. No
signature in §5b was changed. No `Cargo.toml` was touched (§2k).

### Decisions

- 2026-09-17 · phase-1b · **`rpc/jsonrpc.rs` owns one `Transport`, and both clients hold one.**
  §3 lists the file and §2c lists the properties; putting them in one type is what makes them hold
  for both daemons rather than for whichever client was written second (§2a.3's own argument,
  applied to the transport). It carries the `status`-before-payload rule of §1b.3, the
  retry-once-on-401 of §2c under a single mutex, the per-deadline dispatch of §2d and the §2d
  breaker. `RpcError` gained three variants §3 does not enumerate — `Killed`, `Stalled(u64)` and
  `NotRegistered(String)` — because §2d's `killed_at_deadline`, §2d's `stalled` and §1b.2's
  **404 for a gated legacy endpoint** are three different answers that `Transport(String)` would
  have flattened into one. §5b does not freeze that enum.
- 2026-09-17 · phase-1b · **One `ureq::Agent` per client, with the deadline applied per request.**
  §2d says the agent is built once per client with `timeout_global`, and §2d also gives six
  different deadlines. Five agents would be five pooled connections and therefore **five
  server-side Digest sessions** (epee's `m_auth` is per connection, §1b.7), so every call would
  pay a fresh 401. `ureq` 3.4 allows a request-scoped config override
  (`agent.post(url).config().timeout_global(…)`), so the agent's own global timeout is the longest
  any call may take and each request lowers it to its own. One connection, one session, one
  deadline per call.
- 2026-09-17 · phase-1b · **`ureq` is named in exactly one file, and the grep of §2d needs no
  exception list.** `rpc/mod.rs` exports `pub type Agent = ureq::Agent` and one
  `post_once(agent, url, authorization, timeout, body) -> HttpAnswer`; `jsonrpc.rs` speaks only
  fmonero's own types. Without this, `ureq::Error::Timeout` in the error classification would have
  forced `scripts/check.sh` to exempt `jsonrpc.rs`, and a grep with an exemption is a grep that
  stops protecting the file the exemption names.
- 2026-09-17 · phase-1b · **The §2d breaker is consulted at the first request of a client, not at
  construction, and only until that client's first success.** §2a.1 requires steps 1–4 of the
  refusal order to be local, so no constructor may decide a money-path exit code; and §2d says
  `send::relay` must never consult a breaker. A `Plan` can only be relayed on the client that
  built it, and that client has by then completed a request — so "stop consulting after the first
  success" is exactly §2d's rule with no flag to pass and nothing for a caller to remember.
  `status` uses crate-private `NodeClient::for_status` / `WalletClient::for_status`, which neither
  arm nor consult one (§2d), so the frozen API is unchanged and no other caller can pick them.
- 2026-09-17 · phase-1b · **`status` propagates a non-loopback URL and swallows everything else.**
  §2e makes a daemon that is down `reachable: false` and exit 0; §2a's `--force` list requires
  `status --force` with `node.url = http://10.0.0.5:18081` to exit **2**. Both hold because the two
  constructors' only failure is `rpc::require_loopback`, and that one is propagated with `?`.
- 2026-09-17 · phase-1b · **`units::install` refuses to WRITE under `FMONERO_STATE_DIR`, and a dry
  run is allowed to run anywhere.** §4.2 says it "refuses to run at all"; a dry run writes nothing,
  and the §8 gate's own `%h` and `--wallet-dir` lines read the dry run's *output*. Reading the
  literal sentence would have left `tests/e2e.sh` with no way to assert the diff or the `CreateDir`
  action except against the owner's real `~/.config/systemd/user`. The refusal to write is
  asserted by `tests/e2e.sh` (`setup --units --yes` under a test state dir → exit 3).
- 2026-09-17 · phase-1b · **The history snapshot is one postcard struct keyed by account.** §5b
  describes a header and rows; one `history.bin` serves every account of a wallet, so
  `wallet history --account 1` must not discard account 0's rows. The header is the same five
  fields, and `format_version` is bumped on any change to a serialized struct because postcard is
  not self-describing.
- 2026-09-17 · phase-1b · **The in-process tests include the mock's source with `#[path]`.** §8
  Phase 0 asks for one implementation of the request parsing, the Digest server and the request
  log, "shared by making the binary a thin `main()` over a `serve(routes)` function". Sharing it as
  a library needs `[dev-dependencies] fmonero-mock` in `crates/fmonero-core/Cargo.toml`, and no
  manifest may be edited after the 1a gate (§2k). So `crates/fmonero-core/src/rpc/mock_tests.rs`
  includes `../../../fmonero-mock/src/main.rs` and drives it through a new
  `mock::spawn_for_test(argv) -> u16`, which binds an ephemeral port and spawns the same
  `accept_loop` the binary uses. One implementation, no manifest change, and the alternative —
  150 duplicated lines in the crate whose behaviour they are supposed to be testing against — is
  the thing §8 Phase 0 argues against by name.
- 2026-09-17 · phase-1b · **`scripts/check.sh` pairs every `forbid` with a `selftest` that proves
  the same pattern fires on a fixture containing the forbidden thing**, and prints an honest `skip`
  for `shellcheck`, which is not installed (§1.4). It also *counts* the remaining
  `Error::unimplemented` call sites (two: `tui::run` and `doctor::run`, both §8's sanctioned
  handovers) rather than forbidding them, so the number is visible at every run and the Phase 3
  gate has something to compare against.
- 2026-09-17 · phase-1b · **`tests/e2e.sh` gives every case its own state, config and cache
  directory and its own mock**, and `sandbox` kills the previous case's mocks first. The mock ports
  are fixed (18191/18193) because the login file `monero-wallet-rpc` writes is named after its
  **bind port** (§2c), so `wallet.port` and the port must be the same number; that makes killing
  the previous listener a correctness requirement, not tidiness — the first draft read a request log
  belonging to another sandbox and three assertions passed for the wrong reason.

### Design deviations (DESIGN.md is never edited; these are the closest correct thing)

1. **`StateDoc` gained `wallet_behind: Option<i64>`.** §5b lists five fields; §2d's wallet
   exemption is "a wallet call killed at its deadline while `status.wallet.blocks_behind > 100` is
   **`wallet_syncing`, not a stall**", and `blocks_behind` is a number only `status` computes. No
   command on the wallet path has it, and `status` already rewrites this file on every invocation,
   so the last observed value is recorded there and the classification reads it. Without the field
   the exemption is undecidable and the breaker fires during every normal wallet sync — which §2d
   calls "the breaker an agent disables".
2. **`HistoryQuery` gained `pool_unavailable: bool`, written by `query` like its two siblings.**
   §5c's `wallet history` row has a `pool_unavailable` key and §5b gives it no producer: `query`
   returns a plain `Vec<Transfer>`, and "no pool rows" is indistinguishable from "an empty pool".
   (Phase 1a recorded this one too; it is repeated here because Phase 1b is what made it load-bearing.)
3. **`RpcError` gained `Killed`, `Stalled(u64)` and `NotRegistered(String)`**; see the decision
   above. §5b does not freeze that type.
4. **`units::install_rotating` is a second entry point rather than a parameter.** §4.2's
   `--rotate-rpc-login` needs to reach `existing_or_new_node_login(ctx, rotate)`, and
   `install(ctx, dry_run)`'s signature is frozen (§5b).
5. **`crates/fmonero/src/main.rs`'s `refuse_with_plan` was changed by two lines.** `main.rs` is
   frozen at the 1a gate (§2k) and this is the one edit Phase 1b made to it, on the money surface,
   because §5c is explicit and the frozen code contradicted it: the function merged the §5c money
   payload *over* the error envelope, and the payload arrives stamped `ok: true` by `ok()` — so the
   no-`--yes` refusal emitted `ok: true` beside `code: 3`. §5c says "`ok` is `false` and the exit
   code is 3", and `ok` is the first thing an agent branches on. The envelope keys are now
   re-inserted after the merge, so the envelope wins. Reported in the return text as well.
6. **`capabilities_json` gained `"ok": true`.** §4's envelope says every success object carries
   `ok` and `schema`; `capabilities` had only `schema`, and `main.rs` emits the object verbatim.
7. **`session::wallet_session` has no `finish_open`.** §4.1 says `wallet create` "must finish the
   same post-open steps `wallet_session::open` performs"; `main.rs` (frozen) calls the whole
   `wallet_session::open` on the wallet `create_wallet` left open, which re-opens it with its own
   password and then runs those three steps. Adding a second public entry point for them would be a
   frozen method with no caller, which §5b rule 1 forbids.
8. **`tests/e2e.sh` asserts `wallet open` with no unit as exit 9 *and* exit 4.** §4b's GREEN row
   says exit 4; §2b.1 says that with no TTY and neither `--password-file` nor
   `$FMONERO_WALLET_PASSWORD` it exits **9 `wallet_locked`**, and `main.rs` reads the password
   before it contacts anything. Both sentences are true of different inputs, so both are asserted:
   without a password source it is 9, and with `FMONERO_WALLET_PASSWORD` set it is the §4b 4.
9. **`scripts/check.sh` excludes `status.rs` from the `as f64` grep, by name and with the hit
   printed.** §5 types `node.sync_percent` as `f64`; it is a percentage, not a monetary value. The
   exclusion is one file, the check prints the line it excluded at every run, and every other file
   on an amount path is covered.

### Bugs this phase found, in its own work and in Phase 0's

1. **The 401 body was not drained before the retry.** `ureq` returns a connection to the pool only
   once its body has been read, and epee's auth session is **per connection**
   (`http_protocol_handler.h:186`): the retry went out on a fresh socket carrying a nonce the
   server had never issued, took the `stale` path, and failed — so **every authenticated request
   failed, in a way that looks exactly like a wrong password**. Found by the in-process §2c test,
   which is the only thing in the plan that exercises two requests on one nonce.
2. **The mock's static `relay_tx.json` echoed the `transfer` fixture's `tx_hash`,** so §2a.9's
   txid comparison fired on every correct **sweep** (`sweep_all.json` has two transactions with
   their own ids). `relay_tx` now answers the txid whose `tx_metadata` it was handed — which is
   what a real wallet-rpc does (`res.tx_hash = pod_to_hex(get_transaction_hash(ptx.tx))` over the
   `pending_tx` parsed from the request's `hex`, `wallet_rpc_server.cpp:1963`) — and
   `--scenario mismatch` still selects `relay_tx-mismatch.json`, so the check keeps its one test.
3. **The mock wrote its header and its body as two writes.** On a keep-alive connection Nagle holds
   the second segment until the client's ACK of the first: ~40 ms per request on loopback. §2l (b)
   measured **239 ms against an 80 ms budget** — a budget missed by the mock, not by fmonero. One
   `write_all` of header+body plus `set_nodelay(true)`: **55 ms**. A functional test cannot see
   this, and a timing gate that had been "relaxed" here would have hidden fmonero's own real figure.
4. **A forked lock-holder in a test held another test's `send.lock`.** `fork` duplicates
   descriptors into the *same* open file descriptions, so a child that kept them went on holding
   the `flock` — and a test elsewhere failed intermittently with `send_in_progress`. Both fork
   helpers now close every descriptor above stderr before opening anything, using only
   async-signal-safe calls.
5. **`node log` with no log file said "no log yet" only in human mode**, while §4.1 promises the
   sentence. It is now a stderr footer in both modes, like every other §4.1 footer, so the JSON
   payload keeps exactly its §5c key set.

### What is NOT implemented, and who owns it

`crates/fmonero/src/tui/**` and `crates/fmonero/src/doctor/**` are Phase 2's (§8). Their `run`
functions are still `Error::unimplemented` — §8's explicitly sanctioned handover — so `fmonero
doctor` exits 1 today and its §5c key sets (`doctor`, `doctor undo`, `doctor capabilities`) are
asserted by the Phase 2 doctor gate, not by this one. `tests/e2e.sh` records that as a **skip with
the reason**, never as a pass (§2j). `setup --plugin` and `setup --menu` are implemented, but
`plugin/oliver.monero` does not exist until Phase 2, so `--plugin` exits 6 naming the missing
directory rather than guessing a path.

**Carried forward from Phase 0, unresolved and not mine to resolve:** six §7.1 checks
(`units_present`, `unit_loopback`, `unit_no_password_in_argv`, `unit_no_node_wants`,
`wallet_dir_matches_unit`, `wallet_port_consistent`) must read the two unit **files**, and §2i
freezes no override that points them at a fixture directory — `Systemctl::Fixture` answers only
is-active / is-enabled / exists / MainPID. Phase 0's fixtures assume a `FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR`
in `Paths`, honoured only when `FMONERO_STATE_DIR` is set. `paths.rs` is Phase 1a's file and
`probes.rs` is the Phase 2 doctor agent's, so Phase 1b touched neither. Until one resolution is
chosen those six cases cannot run and must be recorded as skipped (§2j).

Every line below was run on 2026-09-17 in this checkout, with **no Monero binary installed**
(§1.1), against a temporary `FMONERO_STATE_DIR`/`FMONERO_CONFIG_DIR`/`FMONERO_CACHE_DIR` wherever a
command writes anything. Nothing here touched the owner's real configuration, units or wallets.
Every daemon answer came from `crates/fmonero-mock` serving fixtures **derived from the Monero
source at v0.18.5.1**, so every row it backs is AMBER: it proves fmonero's wire format, its parsing
and its own sequencing, and nothing about a real daemon (§4b). **No send has ever been broadcast
from this build.**

Two tools §8's gate lines mention are absent on this machine and the honest substitutes are named
where they are used: `hyperfine` and `/usr/bin/time` (so every timing is the 20-iteration
`date +%s%N` loop of §1.4), and `shellcheck` (so `scripts/check.sh` runs `bash -n` and prints a
`skip`).
