### The honesty surfaces, read against §4b

`README.md`'s Install and Status sections, `fmonero version --json`'s `build_note` and
`fmonero robot-docs` were read line by line against the fix-up's §4b matrix. **No false claim was
found, and nothing was edited.** The claims that could be checked, were:

- Install: "run for real, twice in a row, and the second run reported **all seven** of its actions
  `unchanged`" — reproduced above, seven of seven, exit 0 both times.
- Install: `node up`, `node restart` and `wallet open` "exit **4 `not_installed`** within
  milliseconds … and start nothing" — 2 ms, 6 ms, 8 ms, `NRestarts=0`.
- Status GREEN: the `unit_missing` pair, the `not_installed` trio, `setup --units` below the
  120 GB floor (`e2e.sh --full` lines 123-129, including "the message says the floor is an
  estimate"), `wallet send` with no wallet daemon (exit 4 `unreachable`), a bad address with the
  daemon down (exit 15), 13 decimals (exit 2 `bad_amount`), `node down --dry-run` (exit 2
  `unsupported_flag`), `wallet validate`'s structural path (`structural_only: true`), the doctor's
  17 passes, and the 60 ms budget — each reproduced above or in the pasted suite output.
- `build_stage` is `phase-4-verified-and-fixed`; `docs/ROBOT.md` is byte-identical to
  `fmonero robot-docs` (`diff` is empty); `robot-docs` makes no verification claim of its own and
  defers to `build_note`, which is the right shape.
- `capabilities`: 36 commands, 18 exit codes, exactly four accepting `--dry-run`
  (`wallet send`, `wallet sweep`, `setup`, `doctor`), and **14** rows `verified: "red"` — the
  number §4b's lowest-class rule produces on a machine with no Monero.

One residual honesty edge, recorded rather than fixed because it is not a falsehood:
`scripts/uninstall.sh` is a RED row ("not run — `bash -n` only") and README's Install section
describes its behaviour as fact ("the uninstaller refuses `--purge` while a pending-send record
exists, and never deletes your wallets"). Both statements are true of the source
(`scripts/uninstall.sh:136-144`, `:377-399`), and README never says the script has been run, so
the §2j scan is right to pass it — but it is the one place a reader could take an unexercised
script's behaviour for an observed one.

### Defects this verification found, reported and not fixed

All three are in the hint and capability surfaces — the machine-readable half of §4 — and none of
them touches money, secrets or the units. §8 gives a verifier the verification; a bug is reported.

**V1. Three `needs_yes` hints are not runnable: they drop the command's required argument and
exit 2.** `require_yes` (`crates/fmonero/src/main.rs:1952-1958`) builds its hint as
`"{what} --yes"`, and three of its six call sites name a command that takes a required positional:

```
$ FMONERO_WALLET_PASSWORD=x fmonero wallet create zzverify --json
{"code":3,…,"hint":"fmonero wallet create --yes","reason":"needs_yes"}
$ fmonero wallet create --yes --json                                    # running that hint
{"code":2,…,"error":"the following required arguments were not provided: <NAME>","reason":"bad_args"}

$ fmonero wallet book remove 3 --json    → hint "fmonero wallet book remove --yes" → exit 2 <INDEX>
$ fmonero wallet set-daemon 127.0.0.1:18081 --json
                                         → hint "fmonero wallet set-daemon --yes" → exit 2 <ADDRESS>
```

`node down`, `node restart` and `wallet clear-pending` take no positional and their hints do run.
§4's envelope specifies `"hint":"<runnable command>"`, §2g says every error carries a runnable
hint, and README:366 repeats it — so the contract is what the code misses, not the other way
round, which is why nothing was edited in README. The pattern already exists: `send_hint`
(`main.rs:1961-1975`) re-emits the user's own command verbatim for `send`/`sweep`, and
`tests/e2e.sh:864-876` and `:933-938` gate it — a hint that drops `--max-fee` or `--below` fails
the suite. `require_yes` has no such gate, which is why this survived four review passes.
**Fix:** give `require_yes` the user's own argument the way `send_hint` does (three call sites,
about ten lines), and extend the e2e hint assertions from the two money commands to all six.

**V2. `node up`'s `unit_missing` hint is `fmonero setup --units`, which itself exits 3.**

```
$ env FMONERO_STATE_DIR=… FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR=<empty> fmonero node up --json
{"code":4,…,"hint":"fmonero setup --units","reason":"unit_missing"}
$ env … fmonero setup --units --json                                    # running that hint
{"code":3,…,"error":"fmonero setup needs --yes","hint":"fmonero setup --all --yes","reason":"needs_yes"}
```

Two sites: `crates/fmonero-core/src/session.rs:310` and `crates/fmonero-core/src/rpc/node.rs:542`.
The wallet path's equivalent, `units::preflight`, already says `fmonero setup --units --yes` — the
two answers sit side by side in the transcript above and differ by one word. §4b's
`node up | no node unit` row asserts the `reason`, not the hint, so the row is still GREEN and
this is cosmetic where V1 is not. Worth noting alongside it: `setup --units` refused without
`--yes` hints `fmonero setup --all --yes`, which *widens* what the user asked for from `--units`
to `--all` — harmless on an idempotent `setup`, but it is not the user's own command.

**V3. `capabilities` reports `needs_yes: false` for `wallet book`, and `wallet book remove` needs
`--yes`.** §4.1's Y column for that row reads "on remove"; the generated row flattens it:

```
$ fmonero capabilities --json | jq -c '.commands[]|select(.name=="wallet book")'
{"accepts_dry_run":false,"args":"list | add ADDRESS DESC | remove INDEX","mutating":true,
 "name":"wallet book","needs_node":false,"needs_wallet":true,"needs_yes":false,
 "output_format":"json","verified":"amber"}
$ fmonero wallet book remove 3 --json
{"code":3,…,"error":"fmonero wallet book remove needs --yes","reason":"needs_yes"}
```

This is the surface robot-docs tells an agent to branch on, so a wrong `false` there costs a
round trip and an exit 3 on a mutating command. `wallet notes` (`set`) and `node limit` (with
arguments) are correct — neither is `--yes`-gated. **Fix:** either split the `wallet book` row per
subcommand in `agent.rs`'s `COMMANDS`, or make `needs_yes` a tri-state the way the `args` string
already spells the subcommands out.

**Carried forward, unchanged and not re-litigated:** `scripts/uninstall.sh` is still RED
(`bash -n` only); `setup`'s refusal under Arch's `system_unit` layout is still the one scenario
that keeps `setup` a RED command; the 120 GB floor is still an estimate.

Both units **inactive** and **disabled**, `NRestarts=0`, `Result=success`; the two unit files and
`monerod.conf` unchanged by this session; `~/.local/bin/fmonero` the same binary
`install.sh` found; `~/.config/omarchy/shell.json` untouched (both `omarchy` steps were skipped,
not re-run); the working tree clean apart from this entry; no wallet file created, no wallet
opened, no daemon started, no `sudo`, no push.

### Every RED row is still RED — the exact owner commands that turn them GREEN

Unchanged from §4b and from the fix-up entry, on **stagenet** first, because stagenet coins are
free from a faucet and the send path should be exercised the first time with nothing at stake.
The one footnote that still applies: `scripts/install.sh --yes` has now been run twice more and
is a no-op apart from a rebuild.

```bash
omarchy pkg add monero                        # installs monerod + monero-wallet-rpc (needs the owner's password)
scripts/install.sh --yes                      # builds, installs to ~/.local/bin, setup --all (a no-op now)
fmonero settings set node.network stagenet    # ports 38080/38081/38083, wallets/stagenet
fmonero setup --units --yes                   # rewrites both units + monerod.conf (0600), stagenet
fmonero node up --wait --wait-timeout 86400   # expect: exit 0 once synchronized, or exit 8
fmonero wallet create stagetest --yes         # prompts twice, refuses an empty password
monero-wallet-cli --wallet-file ~/.local/share/fmonero/wallets/stagenet/stagetest --offline
                                              # then type: seed — WRITE IT DOWN BEFORE FUNDING
fmonero wallet open stagetest                 # prompts for the password, no echo
grep -c "$YOUR_TEST_PASSWORD" \
  ~/.local/state/fmonero/run/monero-wallet-rpc.log          # expect: 0  (§4.2 --log-level 0)
fmonero wallet address --qr                   # fund this from a stagenet faucet
fmonero wallet history --limit 5 --json
fmonero wallet send <addr> 0.01 --dry-run     # expect: a real fee, nothing relayed
fmonero wallet send <addr> 0.01 --yes         # THE row that matters; then check the txid in history
fmonero wallet send <addr> 0.01 --yes         # run it TWICE: proves the §2a.4 record self-clears
fmonero node restart --yes                    # RED row: a real restart against monerod
fmonero node down --yes                       # RED row: stop_daemon then systemctl --user stop
fmonero node up --wait --wait-timeout 3600    # RED row: a real start
S=$(date +%s%N); for i in $(seq 20); do fmonero status --json >/dev/null; done; E=$(date +%s%N)
echo "warm status mean: $(( (E-S)/20000000 ))ms"   # the §2l (c) figure; paste into DECISIONS.md
fmonero doctor --json | jq '.summary'
tests/e2e.sh --full
# then, only once every row above passed, repeat on mainnet:
fmonero settings set node.network mainnet && fmonero setup --units --yes
```

### What this verification did not do

It did not fix V1, V2 or V3, install Monero, start a daemon, create or open a wallet, broadcast
anything, run `sudo`, or push. It did not edit `README.md`, `agent.rs` or `docs/ROBOT.md`: it went
looking for a false claim in them and did not find one. It could not move the two unit files aside
before `install.sh --yes`, so the idempotence evidence above is the second run's seven-of-seven
`unchanged` plus the unit checksums, not a written-then-unchanged pair — said plainly rather than
rounded up.


## Hint fix-up (real output)

The three defects the previous entry found and deliberately did not fix — **V1** (medium),
**V2** (cosmetic) and **V3** (low-medium) — fixed on 2026-09-17 in
`/home/oliver/Projects/odcpw/fmonero` on `main`, in three commits:

```
9b42f3f  core: the unit_missing hint names a setup command that runs                (V2)
24ea6eb  agent: capabilities splits wallet book, so needs_yes is true on remove      (V3)
bc4b117  cli: every needs_yes hint is the user's own command, and a test runs it     (V1)
```

All three are in the hint and capability surfaces — the machine-readable half of §4 — and none
of them touches money, secrets or the units. **No Monero binary is installed and `sudo` needs a
password** (§1.1), so every §4b RED row is still RED and nothing below claims one. Nothing here
broadcast a transaction, started a `monerod` or a `monero-wallet-rpc`, opened or created a wallet
file, ran `sudo`, or pushed. **No §5b signature and no `Cargo.toml` changed.** `docs/ROBOT.md` is
untouched because `ROBOT_GUIDE` is untouched — `e2e.sh`'s byte-comparison against
`fmonero robot-docs` passes on the tracked file, 69 lines. The one DESIGN.md edit is §2i's layout
table, which carried V2's short hint as the contract.

### V1 — the refusal hint is the user's own command, and running it now reaches the machine

`require_yes` built `"{what} --yes"` and dropped the positional. It now appends `--yes` to the
user's own arguments, the way `send_hint` already did for `send`/`sweep`. The three commands the
previous entry named, in a sandboxed `FMONERO_STATE_DIR` / `FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR` /
`XDG_DATA_HOME`, with the hint of each refusal then run verbatim (`code` is the exit code, §4):

```
$ fmonero wallet create zzhint --json
{"code":3,"reason":"needs_yes","hint":"fmonero wallet create zzhint --yes"}
$ fmonero wallet create zzhint --yes --json                       # running that hint
{"code":4,"reason":"unit_missing","error":"fmonero-wallet-rpc.service is not installed in <sandbox>/systemd"}

$ fmonero wallet book remove 3 --json
{"code":3,"reason":"needs_yes","hint":"fmonero wallet book remove 3 --yes"}
$ fmonero wallet book remove 3 --yes --json                       # running that hint
{"code":4,"reason":"unreachable","error":"monero-wallet-rpc is not answering: io: Connection refused (os error 111)"}

$ fmonero wallet set-daemon 127.0.0.1:18081 --json
{"code":3,"reason":"needs_yes","hint":"fmonero wallet set-daemon 127.0.0.1:18081 --yes"}
$ fmonero wallet set-daemon 127.0.0.1:18081 --yes --json          # running that hint
{"code":4,"reason":"unreachable","error":"monero-wallet-rpc is not answering: io: Connection refused (os error 111)"}

$ fmonero wallet list --json
{"dir":"<sandbox>/data/fmonero/wallets/mainnet","ok":true,"schema":1,"wallets":[]}
```

**Exit 4, not exit 2** — the hint now fails on the machine (no unit, no daemon) instead of on its
own argv, which is the whole defect. No wallet file was created by either invocation: the refusal
is before the password prompt and the run stops at §2i's preflight.

Every flag the user passed comes back too, for the reason `send_hint` gives — a hint that drops a
bound is a different command — so `wallet set-daemon <addr> --trusted` hints
`… --trusted --yes`, and `wallet create` carries `--allow-empty-password` and `--password-file`
when they were given. `--language` is omitted when it is the default clap filled in, because a
hint is read as well as run.

`node down`, `node restart` and `wallet clear-pending` were already runnable and are unchanged in
behaviour; they are now gated by the same test and the same six e2e rows.

### V2 — every `unit_missing` hint names a `setup` command that itself runs

Two sites, `session.rs` (`node up`/`down`/`restart`) and `rpc/node.rs` (`NodeClient::mutators`),
said `fmonero setup --units`, which is itself an exit 3 `needs_yes`. And `setup --units` refused
without `--yes` by hinting `fmonero setup --all --yes`, widening what the user asked for. Both
now name the user's own command:

```
$ fmonero node up --json
{"code":4,"reason":"unit_missing","error":"no fmonero node unit is installed","hint":"fmonero setup --units --yes"}
$ fmonero setup --units --json
{"code":3,"reason":"needs_yes","error":"fmonero setup needs --yes","hint":"fmonero setup --units --yes"}
```

`SetupArgs::own_command` rebuilds the invocation from the flags that were passed, so
`setup --plugin` hints `fmonero setup --plugin --yes` and not `--all`. The **bad-args** refusal
(no target flag at all) still hints `fmonero setup --all --yes`, on purpose: there the user named
no target for `own_command` to echo back. DESIGN.md §2i's layout table, which is where the short
form was written down as the contract, is corrected in the same commit.

### V3 — `capabilities` splits `wallet book`, so `needs_yes` is true exactly where `--yes` is

§4.1's Y column for that row reads "on remove" and one bool per row cannot say it. The row is
split per subcommand exactly as `settings list|get|set` already is — the smaller of the two fixes
the verifier named, and the one that keeps `needs_yes` the boolean an agent branches on and the
§5c key set unchanged:

```
$ fmonero capabilities --json | jq -c '.commands[]|select(.name|startswith("wallet book"))'
{"accepts_dry_run":false,"args":"","mutating":false,"name":"wallet book list","needs_node":false,
 "needs_wallet":true,"needs_yes":false,"output_format":"json","verified":"amber"}
{"accepts_dry_run":false,"args":"ADDRESS DESC","mutating":true,"name":"wallet book add",
 "needs_node":false,"needs_wallet":true,"needs_yes":false,"output_format":"json","verified":"amber"}
{"accepts_dry_run":false,"args":"INDEX","mutating":true,"name":"wallet book remove",
 "needs_node":false,"needs_wallet":true,"needs_yes":true,"output_format":"json","verified":"amber"}

$ fmonero capabilities --json | jq -c '[.commands[]|select(.needs_yes)|.name]'
["node down","node restart","wallet create","wallet send","wallet sweep","wallet clear-pending",
 "wallet book remove","wallet set-daemon","setup"]
$ fmonero capabilities --json | jq '.commands|length'
38
```

`wallet book list` also stops claiming to be mutating. The table is 38 rows where it was 36, and
both directions of `scripts/check.sh`'s drift check still hold — every name is
`fmonero <name> --help`-able ("38 of them") and every subcommand of the binary is documented.
`docs/ROBOT.md` did not change: the guide is prose and names no command row.

### The four new gates, and the proof that none of them is vacuous

Three unit tests in `crates/fmonero/src/main.rs` and one step in `tests/e2e.sh`:

```
test tests::every_needs_yes_hint_parses_under_clap ... ok      # drives the real clap tree
test tests::every_require_yes_call_site_is_listed_here ... ok  # the table is the source's
test tests::capabilities_needs_yes_matches_the_handlers ... ok # both directions
== §4: every needs_yes hint is the user's own command, and running it gets past clap   (e2e.sh)
```

`every_needs_yes_hint_parses_under_clap` collects every `needs_yes` hint this binary can print —
the six `require_yes` sites, `setup`'s own refusal and both `send_hint` forms — and parses each
through `Cli::try_parse_from` with the hint's own argv. `every_require_yes_call_site_is_listed_here`
greps this file for `require_yes(yes, "…` and fails if the count differs from the table's, so a
seventh call site cannot arrive without a row. Each was re-run against the defect it is about,
which is the only way to know a new test is worth its line count:

```
# require_yes reverted to format!("{what} --yes"):
thread 'tests::every_needs_yes_hint_parses_under_clap' panicked:
  assertion `left == right` failed: fmonero wallet create: the hint is not the user's command
# …and with the equality assertion removed too, so the clap half has to answer:
thread 'tests::every_needs_yes_hint_parses_under_clap' panicked:
  the hint `fmonero wallet create --yes` does not parse: error: the following required arguments
  were not provided:
# `wallet book remove` flipped back to needs_yes: false in COMMANDS:
thread 'tests::capabilities_needs_yes_matches_the_handlers' panicked:
  `wallet book remove` refuses without --yes, and capabilities says needs_yes: false
# and the e2e step, against a release binary built from the pre-fix require_yes:
  FAIL  §4: the hint is not the user's own command with --yes appended
        want: fmonero wallet create zzhint --yes
        got:  fmonero wallet create --yes
  FAIL  §4: the hint fails on its own argv, not on the machine
        exit 2, reason bad_args
  FAIL  and the hint's own exit is the machine's answer: expected exit 4, got 2
e2e.sh (fast): 380 passed, 9 failed, 2 skipped
```

Nine failures, three per broken hint, and the tree was restored and rebuilt immediately
afterwards (`git status` clean, `e2e.sh --fast` 389/0 again). The e2e step itself reads:

```
== §4: every needs_yes hint is the user's own command, and running it gets past clap
  ok    node down without --yes (exit 3)
  ok    needs_yes (reason needs_yes)
  ok    §4: the hint is the user's own command — fmonero node down --yes
  ok    §4: `fmonero node down --yes` gets past clap (exit 4, reason unit_missing)
  ok    and the hint's own exit is the machine's answer (exit 4)
  …one such group per command…
  ok    §4: `fmonero wallet clear-pending --yes` gets past clap (exit 0, reason null)
  ok    §4: `fmonero wallet book remove 3 --yes` gets past clap (exit 4, reason unreachable)
  ok    §4: `fmonero wallet set-daemon 127.0.0.1:18081 --yes` gets past clap (exit 4, reason unreachable)
  ok    §4: `fmonero wallet create zzhint --yes` gets past clap (exit 4, reason unit_missing)
  ok    and neither invocation created a wallet file
  ok    node up with no unit installed (exit 4)
  ok    §4: the unit_missing hint runs, rather than refusing in turn
  ok    setup --units without --yes (exit 3)
  ok    §4: and setup hints the user's own command, not --all
```

The two `e2e.sh` skips are still `tui` and `robot-docs`, the two rows §5c marks exempt. The §2j
corpus grew from 1477 to **1505** lines because `capabilities` now lists three `wallet book`
subcommands and `check.sh` reads the `--help` of every row it lists; the scan still finds no
unmarked RED claim, with all four of its self-tests firing. `§5c coverage` now compares 36
commands rather than 34 — `wallet book list`, `wallet book add` and `wallet book remove` each
already had their own `expect_keys` assertion, so the split arrived with its coverage intact.

### The machine, before and after

```
$ systemctl --user show -p ActiveState,UnitFileState,NRestarts fmonero-monerod.service fmonero-wallet-rpc.service
before everything   ActiveState=inactive  UnitFileState=disabled  NRestarts=0   (both units)
before e2e --fast   ActiveState=inactive  UnitFileState=disabled  NRestarts=0   (both units)
after  e2e --fast   ActiveState=inactive  UnitFileState=disabled  NRestarts=0   (both units)
before e2e --full   ActiveState=inactive  UnitFileState=disabled  NRestarts=0   (both units)
after  e2e --full   ActiveState=inactive  UnitFileState=disabled  NRestarts=0   Result=success
```

`NRestarts` never left 0 and neither unit was ever loaded, started or enabled by this session. No
`setup` was run for real: the new e2e step asserts the `setup --units --yes` hint by reading it,
not by running it, because a real `setup --units` calls `systemctl --user daemon-reload` and
`systemctl --user disable` on the **owner's** unit names, which is a side effect this suite does
not take. That is the one claim in this entry that is a comparison rather than an execution, and
it is said here rather than glossed.

### What this entry did not do

It did not install Monero, start a daemon, create or open a wallet, broadcast anything, run
`sudo`, or push. It did not touch `README.md`, `docs/ROBOT.md`, any §5b signature, any
`Cargo.toml`, any unit file, `monerod.conf`, `~/.local/bin/fmonero` or
`~/.config/omarchy/shell.json`. **Carried forward, unchanged and not re-litigated:** every §4b
RED row is still RED and the owner command list that turns them green is the previous entry's,
unchanged; `scripts/uninstall.sh` is still RED (`bash -n` only); `setup`'s refusal under Arch's
`system_unit` layout is still the one scenario that keeps `setup` a RED command; the 120 GB floor
is still an estimate; and README's Install section still describes `uninstall.sh`'s behaviour from
its source rather than from a run — the honesty edge the verifier recorded and did not fix.

- 2026-09-17 · design · DESIGN.md v5.2: remote node mode. The orchestrator fixed the mode model
  (`node.mode`/`node.remote`/`node.remote_trusted`), the two convenience commands, the unit and
  `set_daemon` shapes, the refresh-based send gate, the doctor and TUI/widget treatments, the
  README brief and "no §2c change, no `Cargo.toml` change". Everything below is a decision this
  pass made on top of that list, each recorded because a later agent would otherwise have to
  re-derive it. **Source findings that changed the design** (new §1b.9, all read in the v0.18.5.1
  tree): (a) **`set_daemon` never opens a socket** — it is `m_http_client->set_server(...)`
  (`wallet2.cpp:1409`), a URL parse (`abstract_http_client.cpp:134-148`) — so a successful
  `set_daemon` proves nothing about reachability, and its `-38 "Unable to set daemon"`
  (`wallet_rpc_server.cpp:4758-4763`) means the address did not *parse*. §4.2's v5 parenthetical
  ("raised when wallet2 cannot reach the address") was **wrong** and is corrected in place; the
  local-mode swallow survives with the right justification, and in remote mode `-38` is **not**
  swallowed (exit 4, new `reason: remote_address_rejected`). (b) `set_server` **discards the scheme
  and everything after the port**, and fills a missing port with 80/443, never 18081
  (`:139-146`) — so `node.remote` **requires an explicit `:port`** and refuses a path, rather than
  guessing. (c) `--daemon-ssl autodetect` is **opportunistic and unauthenticated**: verification is
  `system_ca` but the handshake does not fail on a bad certificate (`net_ssl.h:121-124`) and a
  failed handshake permanently downgrades the connection to plaintext (`net_helper.h:225-233`);
  `enabled` throws at startup without a CA/fingerprint/allow-any-cert (`wallet2.cpp:416-432`), so
  autodetect is **forced**, not preferred, and README must say "unauthenticated" in those words.
  (d) giving **neither** trust flag is not "untrusted": `make_basic` falls back to
  `is_local_address(daemon_address)` (`:451-462`), so exactly one of the two flags is now rendered
  **in both modes**. (e) `trusted` leaks the wallet's own unspent amounts *into a request*
  (`select_available_outputs_from_histogram`, `:12281-12282`) — the concrete reason a public node
  is never trusted. (f) **no wallet-RPC method reports the daemon's height or sync state**
  (`on_getheight` is the wallet's own, `wallet_rpc_server.cpp:943`), which is why `node.reachable`
  is `null` in remote mode rather than `false`. **Decisions beyond the orchestrator's list:**
  §1b.9 added as the source section every other change cites, so nothing is re-read. The address
  grammar made explicit with seven refusal rows, each with its `file:line` reason; port mandatory;
  path and userinfo refused; a loopback `node.remote` allowed. A settings **invariant** —
  `mode == remote` ⇒ `node.remote` valid — enforced in `Settings::set` in both directions (new
  exit-2 `reason: remote_unset`), so no consumer needs an "empty address" branch. `node local`
  **keeps** `node.remote`/`node.remote_trusted` rather than clearing them. `settings set` stays
  inert (no systemd, no re-render); the drift it can produce is exactly what the new
  `wallet_daemon_matches_unit` check reports. `--dry-run` placed **before** the `--yes` gate on
  both commands (as `setup --dry-run` already is), which raises the documented `--dry-run` command
  count from four to six. `send::build`'s node argument became `NodeGate{Local,Unreachable,Remote}`
  rather than a re-documented `Option`, because `None` already means the opposite of what remote
  mode needs. The remote gate's deadline miss is **exit 10 `node_syncing`** with the new
  `reason: wallet_catching_up` — not exit 5 — because the caller's action is identical to local
  mode's ("wait, then retry"); exit 10 gained a `reason` in both modes as a result
  (`node_not_synchronized`). §2d amended: **in remote mode no wallet deadline kill arms the
  breaker**, because `blocks_behind` — its only discriminator — is unobservable there.
  `ssl_support` derived from the **address** (loopback ⇒ `disabled`, else `autodetect`) rather than
  from the mode, so local mode, a loopback `node.remote` and `wallet set-daemon` all follow one
  rule; this also fixes a pre-existing waste — our own local unit and `set_daemon` were inheriting
  `autodetect` and attempting TLS against `127.0.0.1` on every reconnect, so **both units now
  render `--daemon-ssl` explicitly** (a `units_present` `unit_drift` rewrite, the documented
  upgrade path). New §5b members beyond the two commands: `units::daemon_target` +
  `ssl_for_address` (six callers of one derivation), `wallet_session::refresh` (three callers of
  "refresh then record"), `state::note_refresh`, `Settings::parse_remote_address`, and the enums
  `NodeMode`/`SslSupport`. §5 gained `node.trusted` (a privacy fact about the running system, not a
  preference), `wallet.last_refresh_at` (the only freshness signal left when `blocks_behind` is
  null — and named "fmonero issued" because the daemon's own 20 s auto-refresh is invisible to us),
  and `node.reachable: Option<bool>`; `node.url` now means "the address in force" in both modes.
  Two new warnings, `remote_node` (info) and `remote_node_trusted` (warn); nine existing node
  warnings documented as never firing in remote mode because `null` is not `false`.
  `monerod_installed` **warns** rather than fails in remote mode, and `wallet_daemon_link`,
  `disk_space` and `prune_configured` were added to the skip set the orchestrator started;
  `unit_loopback` explicitly does **not** change. `remote_node_untrusted`'s private-address list
  includes CGNAT 100.64/10 (Tailscale) and states that a **DNS name is never treated as private**,
  because resolving one inside a §7 pure detector is a network call whose answer can change between
  runs. `node info` is the one node command that still exits 0 and takes a settings-only path
  (never `NodeClient::from_ctx`), with the **same §5c key set** as local mode and four `null`
  sub-objects, so one parser serves both. `wallet set-daemon` gained `ssl_support` and
  `persistent: false` in its payload and now validates its ADDRESS against the same grammar.
  §6 budgets raised deliberately and in this document first (`MoneroIcon.qml` 80→95,
  `Model.js` 150→165, total 1 310→1 340) with the two new icon rows placed **above** the node rows,
  because `!nodeUp`/`!synced` read fields that are null in remote mode and would draw a crossed
  glyph and a 0 % ring for a wallet that is working. The TUI deliberately does **not** offer the
  mode switch — it toasts `fmonero node local --yes` — because the switch restarts a daemon and
  closes the wallet the session is holding. §4b gained a fourth class, **LIVE**: six read-only
  wallet rows (`create`/`open`/`refresh`/`balance`/`address`/`history`, empty wallet) that remote
  mode moves out of RED against a real public node, with a rehearsal that ends in
  `fmonero node local --yes` so the suite leaves the machine as it found it. Nothing in §2c, §2k or
  any `Cargo.toml` changed; §5b.1 lists all nine signature changes in one table, and §8 records
  that v5.2 is a post-Phase-4 amendment run by one agent against the Phase 3 gate list.

- 2026-09-17 · implementer · **the v5.2 review, integrated into DESIGN.md before a line of code was
  written.** Nine revisions came back against the v5.2 amendment — one blocker, four major, four
  minor. **All nine are applied**; none is rejected, and the two whose own `file:line` citations
  were off were applied with corrected numbers rather than argued with, because the substance held
  in both. Every citation below was re-read in the v0.18.5.1 tree of §1.6 before the edit was made.
  - **Blocker — §5b's `wallet_session::open` still carried the v5 contract.** Its doc comment said
    the `set_daemon` arguments come from `Login::from_monerod_conf` and that "a `-38` from
    `set_daemon` is a warning, not a failure", full stop — the **opposite** of what §2m.3 and §4.2
    require in remote mode, in the one module Phase 1b implements from, with §5b.1 closing "nothing
    else in §5b moves". Rewritten: the four arguments come from `units::daemon_target(ctx)`, `login`
    is `None` **always** in remote mode, `ssl` is address-derived, and the `-38` rule is split by
    mode (swallowed locally; exit 4 `backend`, `reason: remote_address_rejected` remotely).
    §5b.1 is now **ten** amendments, not nine: #10 is this behaviour change, listed for the reason
    #9 already gives — a signature that does not move is not a contract that did not move, and a
    Phase 2 agent compiling against §5b gets no warning that `open` can now fail for a new reason.
  - **Major — decoy-set manipulation was missing from §2m.6's privacy list.** The node supplies the
    material every ring is built from: `get_rct_distribution` fills `rct_offsets`
    (`wallet2.cpp:9258-9261`) with only a monotonicity check (`:9269`) and a suspicious-count bound
    (`:9271`), `gamma_picker` is seeded from exactly those offsets (`:9402`), and the ring members
    come from `/get_outs.bin` (`:9736`) — all verified. A fourth bullet says so, and says the thing
    that makes it worth its own line: **`trusted: false` does not reduce it**, so a list that ends
    at IP, timing and (when trusted) amounts understates the risk on the axis people choose a node
    for. The same sentence is in §2m.7 point 3, in the same words, so the two texts cannot drift.
  - **Major — the fee lie was pooled with the two lies that cost nothing.** §2m.6 named three lies
    and gave all three one consequence ("exit 16, funds unaffected"). An inflated fee estimate is
    the one that costs real money and the only one with no automatic detection:
    `get_base_fee(priority)` returns the daemon's `get_dynamic_base_fee_estimate_2021_scaling`
    answer verbatim (`wallet2.cpp:8584-8608`, the call at `:8597`) and **every** build path uses it
    — `:11160`, `:11807`, `:11924`, each confirmed to read `get_base_fee(priority)` — while
    `adjust_priority` (`:8688`) also reads the daemon's pool backlog. The transaction relays
    successfully and the excess goes to a miner. The paragraph is now three bullets with three
    consequences, and both §2m.6 and §2m.7 point 3 name **`--max-fee`** (§2a.2), which this tool
    already ships and which §2m had never mentioned.
  - **Major — nothing said that the remote node picks the new wallet's restore height.**
    `on_create_wallet` builds the wallet2 from the *process's* `--daemon-address`
    (`wallet_rpc_server.cpp:3596`), then `invoke_http_json("/getheight", …)` and
    `set_refresh_from_block_height(hres.height)` with `hres.height` initialised to 0 and left there
    when the call fails (`:3606-3609`) — **before** fmonero's `set_daemon` runs. (The reviewer cited
    `:3602-3607`; the lines are `:3606-3609` and `make_new` is `:3596`. Corrected, not disputed.)
    New paragraph in §2m.3, a clause on §4.1's `wallet create` row, a sentence in §2m.7 point 1 and
    an assertion in §4b's LIVE rehearsal: a `wallet refresh` whose `blocks_fetched` is a genesis
    scan is `wallet create` having silently taken restore height 0 from a node that did not answer,
    and §0 cuts both `rescan_blockchain` and `scan_tx`, so v0.1 ships no recovery —
    `monero-wallet-cli`'s `rescan_bc` is the way out and README says so.
  - **Major — `node remote` / `node local` restarted `monero-wallet-rpc` with neither money guard.**
    They now take the §2a.11 `send.lock` and run the §2a.4 `pending_send` entry check, as steps 3
    and 4 of what is now **nine** steps. The reason is sharper than symmetry: this is the one
    command whose purpose is to change the node a send is resolved against, so §2a.4's resolve step
    would run `get_transfer_by_txid` against a **different** node, get "not found" for a transaction
    that really was broadcast, and block every later send until the user typed `clear-pending` — the
    habit §2a.4 exists to prevent. **`--dry-run` is exempt, explicitly**, taking the second of the
    two options the review offered: it exits at step 2, writes nothing, restarts nothing and can
    abort no relay, so blocking a preview would refuse the safe half of the command for a risk it
    does not carry — the same argument step 2 already makes for putting `--dry-run` ahead of
    `--yes`. §4.1's `--yes` justification names both guards; §4b gains three GREEN rows for them
    (both commands refusing on an unrelayed record, the `--dry-run` exemption still exiting 0, and
    a held `send.lock` giving `send_in_progress`), none of which needs a daemon.
  - **Minor — §2m.3 and §7.1 disagreed about which `node` checks skip in remote mode.** §2m.3 named
    one exception (`node_layout`); §7.1 named two. The second is `remote_node_untrusted`, the only
    check in the document that is meaningful *only* in remote mode, so the §2m wording would have
    disabled the trusted-public-node warning in the one mode it can fire. §2m.3 now names both.
  - **Minor — the `ftp://` refusal's stated reason was false.** "Anything else parses to port 0 and
    connects to port 0" is not what the source does: `parse_url`'s regex captures an explicit port
    in group 6 whatever the scheme (`net_parse_helpers.cpp:181`, `:199-203`) and the 80/443 table
    runs only `if (parsed.port == 0)` (`abstract_http_client.cpp:139-144`), after which only host
    and port are forwarded (`:146`) — which is §1b.9 item 1's own "the scheme is discarded", in the
    same document. The row and its exit code are unchanged; the rationale is now the true one (with
    the port mandatory the scheme has **no** effect, so accepting `ftp://` would silently discard
    it — the §2j failure the path row already refuses).
  - **Minor — §1b.9 item 4's "five things" stopped at one translation unit.**
    `monero-wallet-rpc` gates five more handlers on `is_trusted_daemon()`, all confirmed:
    `import_key_images` (`wallet_rpc_server.cpp:3151`), `rescan_spent` (`:3456`) and `start_mining`
    (`:3484`) refuse with "This command requires a trusted daemon."; `import_multisig`'s post-import
    `rescan_spent` is skipped (`:4432`) and the background-mining check returns early (`:438`). All
    five are cut by §0, so nothing breaks today — and all five are where a v0.2 restoring one of
    them will meet the flag. The count now reads "five in `wallet2` and five more in wallet-RPC".
  - **Minor — an off-by-two.** `wallet2.cpp:447-448` is the conditional **assignment**; the
    `THROW_WALLET_EXCEPTION_IF` that makes two trust flags fatal is `:449-450`. Both citations
    (§1b.9 item 3 and §4.2) corrected.

  Nothing was rejected, so there is no evidence to record against one. Two things the review did
  **not** move and this pass did not move either: §2c is untouched (no TLS crate, no remote call
  from fmonero, `require_loopback` unchanged), and no `Cargo.toml` changes (§2k).

## Remote node mode gate (real output)

Run on 2026-09-17 in `/home/oliver/Projects/odcpw/fmonero` on `main`, at the tree this entry
lands with. **Monero 0.18.5.1 is installed on this machine and no daemon has ever been started
here** — that is new since the previous entry and it is what §1.1 now says. Nothing below
started a `monerod` or a `monero-wallet-rpc`, broadcast a transaction, opened or created a wallet
file, ran `sudo`, or pushed. **`fmonero node remote` was never run for real**: every remote-mode
row below is settings, rendering, or a mock, and §4b's LIVE list is untouched — those six rows
stay RED until somebody runs them against a real public node and pastes the output here.

### The five commits

```
b4bda2d  design: v5.2 remote node mode, with the review's nine revisions integrated
7f50d16  core: remote node mode — settings, layout, daemon_target, the send gate
b3d5032  tui, plugin, docs: the remote-mode surfaces
360282a  tests, docs: the remote-mode cases, the README section, and an flock fix
```

(`0064093`, a docs/sessions log, landed between them from another session and touches no file
this work owns.)

### The gate

  ok    and every subcommand the binary offers is documented

`NRestarts` never left 0, neither unit was loaded, started or enabled, and no `setup --units` was
run for real. The two `e2e --full` failures are §2l timings and they are **not** hidden: see
below.

### The two failing assertions, and the A/B that says whose they are

```
  FAIL  §2l (a) status, both daemons absent: 70ms mean, over the 60 ms budget
  FAIL  §2l (b) status against the mock: 88ms mean, over the 80 ms budget
```

They fail on the **pre-v5.2 binary too**, on this machine, today. `e54c8bd` — the commit the
previous gate was run at — was built in a scratch worktree and measured against this tree's
binary back to back, in the same sandbox, minutes apart:

```
$ uptime
 20:02:05 up 2 days, load average: 3.62, 3.18, 2.90
e54c8bd (pre-v5.2): 77ms mean
HEAD    (+v5.2):    74ms mean
$ two `systemctl --user is-active` spawns: 21-23ms mean      (19ms when §2l was written)
```

So the change is, if anything, **three milliseconds faster**, and the miss is the machine: the
owner's own desktop carries a standing load average near 3.5 (ghostty, two Electron apps,
quickshell), and the two `systemctl` spawns §2l calls "the expensive part" now cost 21–23 ms for
the pair where they cost 19 ms on 2026-09-16.

**The budget is not relaxed and the assertion is not weakened.** §2l's own paragraph says a GREEN
budget set below its floor "is a number that gets quietly relaxed on first contact"; relaxing it
here, in the commit that fails it, is exactly that. It is recorded with the real number, which is
what §2l asks for, and it is reported as an open item rather than as a pass. One v5.2 change did
touch this path and was fixed on the way: `status` briefly read `state.json` a second time for
`wallet.last_refresh_at`, and now takes it from the document it already loads for `last_error`.

### A pre-existing intermittent failure, found and reported rather than papered over

`cargo test -p fmonero-core` fails roughly **3 runs in 12** with a spurious exit-3
`send_in_progress` out of `send::take_send_lock`, in a different test each time
(`the_recorded_bodies_…`, `the_refusal_order_…`, `a_fee_above_max_…`). It is **pre-existing**:

```
$ git stash && for i in $(seq 8); do cargo test -p fmonero-core; done     # at the pre-v5.2 tree
test rpc::mock_tests::a_fee_above_max_refuses_and_relays_nothing ... FAILED     (1 of 8)
```

What is now known about it, so the next agent does not start over:

* any of those tests run **alone, 25 times: 25 passes**;
* the whole suite with `-- --test-threads=1`: **5 runs, 5 passes** (and 33 s instead of 13 s);
* every test has its own state directory, so no two share a `send.lock` path, and no two
  `World::new` names are duplicated;
* instrumenting the failure to dump `/proc/locks` printed `holders=[]` — **no process held a
  lock on that inode** at the moment `flock` answered `EWOULDBLOCK`.

One real bug fell out of the hunt and is fixed in `360282a`: `take_send_lock` treated **every**
non-zero `flock` return as "somebody else holds it", **`EINTR` included**. `flock(2)` is
interruptible and this process reaps children on every `proc::run_with_deadline`, so a signal was
enough to refuse a legitimate send with a message naming a holder that does not exist — on the
money path. It now retries on `EINTR`, reports `EWOULDBLOCK`/`EAGAIN` as the interlock, and gives
any other errno exit 1 rather than exit 3, because §4's whole argument for `reason` is that an
agent acts on the difference. That fix did not remove the flake, so the flake is not that bug.
The remainder is filed as a task with the full transcript; the gate runs above are reported as
they happened, including the one `cargo test` run that hit it.

### The DESIGN.md amendments this pass made beyond the review's nine

Each is in §5b.1 (now **twelve** rows) or in the section it changes:

1. **§5b.1 #11** — `units::remote_node_error(ctx, verb)` and `send::refuse_if_send_in_flight`.
   §2m.3 prescribes a per-verb message and `NodeClient::from_ctx` does not know the verb, so the
   constructor builds the plural form and `main.rs` passes the verb it typed; one construction,
   three call layers. The second is §2a.4's entry check **without** the resolve step, which
   `node_mode` must not run against a daemon it is about to restart.
2. **§5b.1 #12** — the four address predicates in `settings` and
   `paths::systemd_user_dir_is_sandboxed`. The last narrows §4.2's "a test never installs a unit"
   by exactly one case: a unit written into `FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR` **while
   `Systemctl::Fixture` is also set**. Either half alone still refuses — and this repo's own e2e
   case asserting that `setup --units --yes` exits 3 under a sandbox still passes, because that
   case sets no fixture.
3. **§1.1 and §4b's GREEN `doctor` row, restated for the machine as it is.** Monero was installed
   here on 2026-09-17, which turned `monerod_installed` and `wallet_rpc_installed` from
   `fail:not_installed` into passes and **broke four `e2e.sh` assertions and one unit test before
   a line of v5.2 code existed**:

   ```
   $ git stash && tests/e2e.sh --fast          # at the pre-v5.2 tree
     FAIL  exactly monerod_installed and wallet_rpc_installed fail (§4b)
           [.checks[] | select(.status=="fail") | .id] = ["node_reachable"]
     FAIL  and both say fail:not_installed, not a bare fail
     FAIL  every node/wallet/storage check is a skip:<reason> (§7.1)
     FAIL  and every one of those skips names its reason, never a bare skip
   e2e.sh (fast): 377 passed, 4 failed, 3 skipped
   $ cargo test -p fmonero                     # at the same tree
   test doctor::checks::tests::the_one_line_predicates_answer_as_the_table_says ... FAILED
   ```

   They are **restated, not relaxed**: the shape asserted is still "exit 11, a named failing set,
   no bare skips", now for a machine where the package is present and nothing is running
   (`node_reachable` is the one `fail`). The unit test now clears `which_monerod` explicitly
   instead of capturing the host — a test that asserts a predicate by probing the machine asserts
   the machine.
4. **§7a's line budget 4 600 → 4 700** (measured 4 655) and **§6.1's 1 310 → 1 340** (measured
   1 305), both stated in DESIGN.md before the code was written, which is the rule §7a's own
   v5.1 paragraph sets. The 98 TUI lines are §2m's Node screen — the remote hero §7a specifies,
   the `u`/`d`/`L` toast, the `?` overlay's marks and the `NodeGate` arm in `subs.rs`. Nothing
   was deleted to fit.

### Deviations recorded under §2k

1. **`node_mode` lives in `src/session.rs`**, beside `wallet_session` and `node_session`, rather
   than in a file of its own. §3's layout line is updated to say so. It is the third sequence
   module and it shares their reason for existing: two independently written callers (the CLI
   and, one day, anything else) must not each compose the nine steps.
2. **`WalletClient::set_daemon` maps `-38` itself**, to exit 4 `reason: remote_address_rejected`,
   instead of letting §1b.4's table turn it into `unreachable`. `set_daemon` opens no socket
   (§1b.9 item 1), so `unreachable` is the one thing that code cannot mean there — and
   `wallet_session::open` needs to tell a rejected address from a wallet daemon that stopped
   answering, which it cannot do from a shared reason. `wallet set-daemon` gets the right answer
   for free.
3. **`status` reads `units::daemon_target` for `node.url` and `node.trusted` in both modes.** §5
   says `node.url` is "the address in force"; deriving it anywhere else would be a second
   statement of the rule §2m.3 puts in one place.
4. **The plugin's remote rows are asserted structurally, not through a Status fixture.**
   `tests/plugin-smoke.sh` has no way to feed the widget a document, and a setter that injected
   one would let anything spoof what the bar displays. The property that matters — the two remote
   icon cases are matched **before** `!svc.nodeUp`, without which the bar draws a crossed glyph
   and a 0 % ring for a wallet that is working — is asserted by comparing their positions in the
   `iconCase` expression, and four `present` checks cover `Model.remoteLabel`, the panel's use of
   it, the inert toggle and the `cursor` field. **The live half of that script reloaded the shell
   and the widget answered, but with the PREVIOUS build of the QML**: `omarchy-shell shell
   rescanPlugins` does not recompile an already-loaded component and this Omarchy exposes no
   `reload` verb, so the QML edits reach the bar at the owner's next shell restart. That is said
   here rather than glossed, and it is why the new assertions are static.
5. **`arm_breaker` returns early in remote mode**, implementing §2d's v5.2 sentence: the wallet
   exemption's only discriminator, `blocks_behind`, is unobservable there.

### What this entry did not do

It did not run `fmonero node remote` for real, contact any public node, install or remove
anything, start a daemon, create or open a wallet file, run `sudo`, or push. It did not touch the
owner's `~/.config/systemd/user`, `~/.config/fmonero`, `~/.local/bin/fmonero` or
`~/.config/omarchy/shell.json`. No `Cargo.toml` changed and no TLS crate entered the tree — §2c's
two compile-time greps still pass, and `NodeClient::from_ctx` refusing in remote mode is now the
enforceable form of "fmonero never speaks to a remote node", asserted by
`no_node_client_can_be_constructed_in_remote_mode`. **Carried forward, unchanged:** §4b's LIVE
rows are RED, every §4b RED row that needs a local `monerod` is still RED, `scripts/uninstall.sh`
is still `bash -n` only, `setup`'s refusal under Arch's `system_unit` layout is still the scenario
that keeps `setup` a RED command, and the 120 GB floor is still an estimate.
